77	## Todo
78	
79	 Prioritized entries (referenced by `## Priorities`) lower priority

86	1. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
87	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
88	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
89	   each side:
90	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
91	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
92	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
93	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
94	     all N code commits
95	
96	   Teach push to:
97	   - detect this shape
98	   - skip `commit-app` when code WC is empty
99	   - compose a `.claude`-specific message
100	   - emit multi-line `ochid:` per the design in [[10]]
101	
102	   Today's workaround: pre-commit `.claude` manually, then
103	   `vc-x1 push <bm> --from bookmark-both --yes`.
104	2. **vc-x1 push --squash: symmetric squash on both repos.**
105	   Automate Option F (manually exercised in 0.59.0
106	   close-out): app-side squash + bot-side description
107	   rewrite + force-push, atomically. Without this,
108	   squash is a manual recipe that future cycles must
109	   follow each time.
110	   - App side: squash cycle commits into one new commit;
111	     capture the squashed chid.
112	   - Bot side: rewrite the prior push commit's
113	     description — replace its per-commit `ochid:`
114	     trailers with one pointing at the squashed chid;
115	     add a rewrite-note acknowledging the change
116	     (preserves historical truth for future readers).
117	   - Force-push bot `main` (rewrites the published
118	     commit; chid preserved via `jj describe`).
119	   - Push app `main`. The new bot commit paired with
120	     this push receives `ochid: /<squashed-chid>` as
121	     normal — produces a 2:1 bot→code pattern that's
122	     already in the protocol's design space.
123	   - Gates `Squash to one commit` as a routine
124	     close-out shape vs. the current manual recipe.
125	3. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**
126	   `0.47.0` introduced the convention (single-field leaf names
127	   its field `value`, declares the flag via `#[arg(long = "…")]`,
128	   so consumers read `args.<leaf>.value` not `args.<leaf>.<leaf>`)
129	   on the new `squash` leaf. Sweep the pre-existing single-field
130	   leaves to match: `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`, `account`,
131	   `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
132	   (`init.rs`, tests).
133	
134	   Note: can a single field be defined as an type or enum instead
135	   of a struct and maybe eliminate the `args.<leaf>.<leaf>` name
136	   issue.
137	4. **`por → dual` conversion.** Attach a `.claude`
138	   companion repo + `.vc-config.toml` to an existing por
139	   workspace; emit cross-links going forward. Manual
140	   setup on an external por workspace (2026-05-14)
141	   proved arduous; this should be a routine subcommand.
142	   Design stub in [[1]] § 2.
143	5. **por/dual parity + `dual → por` conversion.** Make
144	   `por` and `dual` first-class equals (dual is primary
145	   today, por bolted on); add `dual → por` conversion
146	   (detach the `.claude` companion). Builds on the
147	   `--scope` rollout below. Pre-design; goal + open
148	   questions in the stub. [[1]]

P1 is important but actually looking at the Todo 1..5 as well as the Idea 1 they all except Todo 4 revolve around Todo 4 & 5 i.e. we need por/dual parity because then we'll understand the differences and then can do the pos -> dual. So I suggest we start with T5 por/dual parity. We already support `vc-x1 init --por xxx` from -h:
      --por
          Create a plain single repo (no `.claude/` companion)

But the default is it creates .vc-config.toml and it creates the remote repo.
And actually there should be a ~/.config/vc-config.toml which defines the defaults so the user chooses.
So I'm thinking we start with ~/.config/vc-config.toml.
thoughts?

```
1	# por/dual parity + bidirectional conversion
2	
3	Forward-looking design capture (pre-design). Concrete work
4	lands when a cycle picks it up — likely on top of the
5	`--scope` rollout (the `0.50.0` work in `notes/todo.md`).
6	
7	`vc-x1` today treats the **dual** workspace (a code repo
8	plus a `.claude` bot-session repo, cross-linked by `ochid:`
9	trailers, configured by `.vc-config.toml`) as the primary
10	shape; **por** (Plain Old Repo — a single repo, no
11	`.claude/`, no `.vc-config.toml`) is supported but bolted
12	on. Two goals:
13	
14	## 1. Parity
15	
16	por and dual should be first-class equals across every
17	subcommand — same code paths, same quality of support, no
18	"dual plus an afterthought" asymmetry. The `--por` topology
19	flag (dual vs single — see `notes/todo.md`) is the
20	substrate; parity is the goal it serves.
21	
22	## 2. Bidirectional conversion
23	
24	A new capability: convert a workspace between the two
25	shapes in place.
26	
27	- **`por → dual`** — attach a `.claude` companion repo and
28	  a `.vc-config.toml` to an existing single repo; emit
29	  cross-links going forward.
30	- **`dual → por`** — detach the `.claude` companion; drop
31	  `.vc-config.toml`; the code repo continues standalone.
32	
33	## Open questions (resolve at design time)
34	
35	- Surface: a new subcommand (`vc-x1 convert`?
36	  `promote` / `demote`?) vs. flags on existing commands.
37	- `dual → por` — what happens to the `.claude` history:
38	  archived, left in place untouched, deleted? Leading
39	  guess is "left alone, just unlinked," but TBD.
40	- `.vc-config.toml` — creation on `por → dual` (which
41	  fields, defaults from user config?) and removal on
42	  `dual → por`.
43	- Whether `init` / `clone` change, or conversion is purely
44	  a separate operation on an already-existing workspace.
45	- Idempotency / re-running; recovery from a conversion
46	  interrupted mid-way.
47	
48	## See also
49	
50	- `notes/todo.md` — the por/dual item, and the `--scope`
51	  cluster it builds on.
52	- `src/options_flags/por.rs` — `PorFlag`, the `--por`
53	  topology flag.
54	- `notes/chores/chores-07.md > ## init + clone redesign (0.41.1)`
55	  — the `init_one` / `clone_one` primitives that aim to
56	  give a single chokepoint for scope-shaped changes.
57	
```

vc-x1 0.60.0

Create a new dual-repo project

Usage: vc-x1 init [OPTIONS] <TARGET> [NAME]

Arguments:
  <TARGET>  Target — URL, owner/name shorthand, path, or bare NAME.
  [NAME]    Repo directory name override (URL / owner/name forms only).

Options:
      --account <NAME>
          Account name — picks `[account.<a>]` from user config.
  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits
      --repo <CAT[=VAL]>
          Repo target — `<cat>` or `<cat>=<val>`.
  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace
      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)
      --por
          Create a plain single repo (no `.claude/` companion)
      --dry-run
          Dry run — show what would be done without executing
      --private
          Create private GitHub repos (default: public).
      --push-retries <PUSH_RETRIES>
          Max push retries after repo creation [default: 5] [default: 5]
      --push-retry-delay <PUSH_RETRY_DELAY>
          Seconds between push retries [default: 3] [default: 3]
      --use-template <CODE[,BOT]>
          Seed repos from template directories.
      --config <none|PATH>
          Override the canned `.vc-config.toml` write.
  -h, --help
          Print help (see more with '--help')

```
1	//! User-level config loaded from `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`.
2	//!
3	//! Backs init's account/repo-target resolution. No magic
4	//! fallbacks — a missing file or missing key produces a
5	//! predictable error pointing at the exact key to set.
6	//!
7	//! Schema (multi-account, dotted keys for compactness):
8	//!
9	//! ```toml
10	//! [default]
11	//! account = "home"      # default --account when absent
12	//! debug   = "trace"     # default --debug value when used without arg
13	//!
14	//! [account.home]
15	//! repo.default          = "remote"                       # default --repo cat when absent
16	//! repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:winksaville"   # value for --repo remote (no =val)
17	//! repo.category.local   = "~/test-fixtures"              # value for --repo local (no =val)
18	//!
19	//! [account.work]
20	//! repo.default          = "remote"
21	//! repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:anthropic"
22	//! repo.category.local   = "/work/fixtures"
23	//! ```
24	//!
25	//! Three-layer resolution — see
26	//! `notes/chores/chores-08.md > User config (0.41.1-3, redesigned in 0.41.1-4)`:
27	//!
28	//! 1. account: CLI `--account` → `[default].account` → error.
29	//! 2. category: CLI `--repo <cat>` → `[account.<a>.repo].default`
30	//!    → error.
31	//! 3. value: CLI `--repo <cat>=<val>` →
32	//!    `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>` → error.
33	//!
34	//! Values are **literal targets**, not section-name pointers.
35	//! For built-in `category = "remote"` the value is a URL prefix
36	//! (init appends `/<NAME>.git`); for `category = "local"` it's
37	//! the parent dir for fixture bare repos.
38	//!
39	//! `-3` shipped a flat first-cut (`[default] repo-remote-provider`,
40	//! `[github] owner`); `-4` is the redesign before any consumer
41	//! is wired in. `-5` (init reshape) is the first consumer.
42	
43	use std::collections::HashMap;
44	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
45	
46	use log::debug;
47	
48	use crate::toml_simple;
49	
50	/// Per-account configuration.
51	///
52	/// - `repo_default` — `[account.<name>.repo].default` — the
53	///   category to use when `--repo` is absent.
54	/// - `repo_category` — `[account.<name>.repo.category]` — map
55	///   from category name (e.g. `"remote"`, `"local"`) to its
56	///   literal value (URL prefix, fixture parent dir, etc.).
57	#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
58	pub struct AccountConfig {
59	    pub repo_default: Option<String>,
60	    pub repo_category: HashMap<String, String>,
61	}
62	
63	/// Resolved user config.
64	///
65	/// All fields are optional or default-empty — absent file or
66	/// absent keys yield empty values. Consumers apply their own
67	/// errors at use time.
68	///
69	/// **Two write modes** for repo defaults:
70	/// - **Top-level `[repo]`** — single-account shorthand. Sits in
71	///   `top_level_repo`. Used when neither `--account` nor
72	///   `[default].account` resolves to a known account.
73	/// - **`[account.<name>]`** — multi-account. Sits in `accounts`.
74	///   Selected by `--account` CLI or `[default].account`.
75	///
76	/// Mixing both is rejected at load time (ambiguous which one
77	/// init should consult).
78	#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
79	pub struct UserConfig {
80	    /// `[default].account` — default account when `--account`
81	    /// is absent.
82	    pub default_account: Option<String>,
83	
84	    /// `[default].debug` — default value when `--debug` is used
85	    /// without an argument. (Reserved; not currently consumed.)
86	    pub default_debug: Option<String>,
87	
88	    /// Top-level `[repo]` — single-account shorthand. `Some`
89	    /// only when the file has top-level `repo.*` keys and no
90	    /// `[account.*]` sections.
91	    pub top_level_repo: Option<AccountConfig>,
92	
93	    /// Per-account configuration, keyed by account name.
94	    pub accounts: HashMap<String, AccountConfig>,
95	}
96	
97	/// CLI selector for `--repo <cat>[=<val>]`.
98	///
99	/// - `--repo` absent → `None`.
100	/// - `--repo <cat>` → `Some({ category: cat, value: None })`.
101	/// - `--repo <cat>=<val>` → `Some({ category: cat, value: Some(val) })`.
102	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
103	pub struct RepoSelector {
104	    pub category: String,
105	    pub value: Option<String>,
106	}
107	
108	/// Path to the user config file.
109	///
110	/// - Honors `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` if set (XDG Base Directory spec).
111	/// - Falls back to `$HOME/.config`.
112	/// - Errors if neither variable is set.
113	pub fn config_path() -> Result<PathBuf, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
114	    let base = if let Some(xdg) = std::env::var_os("XDG_CONFIG_HOME") {
115	        PathBuf::from(xdg)
116	    } else if let Some(home) = std::env::var_os("HOME") {
117	        PathBuf::from(home).join(".config")
118	    } else {
119	        return Err("neither $XDG_CONFIG_HOME nor $HOME is set".into());
120	    };
121	    Ok(base.join("vc-x1").join("config.toml"))
122	}
123	
124	/// Load the user config from the default location.
125	///
126	/// - Missing file → empty `UserConfig`.
127	/// - Malformed file → fatal error.
128	pub fn load() -> Result<UserConfig, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
129	    load_from(&config_path()?)
130	}
131	
132	/// Load the user config from an explicit path.
133	///
134	/// - Missing file → empty `UserConfig`.
135	/// - Malformed file → fatal error (propagated from `toml_simple`).
136	/// - Unknown sections / keys are silently ignored
137	///   (forward-compatible with future schema additions).
138	pub fn load_from(path: &Path) -> Result<UserConfig, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
139	    if !path.exists() {
140	        debug!(
141	            "config: file not found at {} — using empty UserConfig",
142	            path.display()
143	        );
144	        return Ok(UserConfig::default());
145	    }
146	    debug!("config: loading from {}", path.display());
147	    let map = toml_simple::toml_load(path)?;
148	
149	    let mut cfg = UserConfig {
150	        default_account: map.get("default.account").cloned(),
151	        default_debug: map.get("default.debug").cloned(),
152	        top_level_repo: None,
153	        accounts: HashMap::new(),
154	    };
155	
156	    let mut top_level = AccountConfig::default();
157	    let mut top_level_seen = false;
158	
159	    // Walk all keys; route `repo.*` to top-level, `account.<n>.…`
160	    // to per-account.
161	    for (full_key, value) in &map {
162	        if let Some(suffix) = full_key.strip_prefix("repo.") {
163	            top_level_seen = true;
164	            apply_repo_subkey(&mut top_level, suffix, value);
165	            continue;
166	        }
167	        let Some(rest) = full_key.strip_prefix("account.") else {
168	            continue;
169	        };
170	        let Some((name, suffix)) = rest.split_once('.') else {
171	            continue;
172	        };
173	        let Some(repo_suffix) = suffix.strip_prefix("repo.") else {
174	            continue; // unknown sub-section under [account.<n>]
175	        };
176	        let account = cfg.accounts.entry(name.to_string()).or_default();
177	        apply_repo_subkey(account, repo_suffix, value);
178	    }
179	
180	    if top_level_seen && !cfg.accounts.is_empty() {
181	        return Err(format!(
182	            "{}: mixing top-level [repo] with [account.*] is ambiguous — \
183	             remove top-level [repo] or move it under [account.<name>]",
184	            path.display()
185	        )
186	        .into());
187	    }
188	    if top_level_seen {
189	        cfg.top_level_repo = Some(top_level);
190	    }
191	
192	    debug!(
193	        "config: parsed default_account={:?}, default_debug={:?}, top_level_repo={}, accounts={:?}",
194	        cfg.default_account,
195	        cfg.default_debug,
196	        cfg.top_level_repo.is_some(),
197	        cfg.accounts.keys().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
198	    );
199	    Ok(cfg)
200	}
201	
202	/// Apply one `repo.*` sub-key (e.g. `default`, `category.remote`)
203	/// onto an `AccountConfig`. Used for both the top-level `[repo]`
204	/// path and the `[account.<n>.repo]` path.
205	fn apply_repo_subkey(account: &mut AccountConfig, suffix: &str, value: &str) {
206	    match suffix {
207	        "default" => {
208	            account.repo_default = Some(value.to_string());
209	        }
210	        s if let Some(cat) = s.strip_prefix("category.") => {
211	            account
212	                .repo_category
213	                .insert(cat.to_string(), value.to_string());
214	        }
215	        _ => {} // unknown sub-key; ignore (forward-compat)
216	    }
217	}
218	
219	/// Resolve `(category, value)` for an init invocation.
220	///
221	/// Three-step chain — each step has its own error message
222	/// pointing at the exact config key to set or CLI arg to pass.
223	///
224	/// - `account_override` — `Some(a)` from `--account a` CLI;
225	///   `None` falls back to `[default].account`.
226	/// - `repo_cli` — the parsed `--repo` selector (or `None` if
227	///   the flag was absent).
228	///
229	/// Fast path: when `repo_cli` provides both `category` and
230	/// `value` (`--repo <cat>=<val>`), the result is fully
231	/// determined by the CLI; no account or category lookup is
232	/// needed and account-related errors are skipped. This makes
233	/// config-less invocations (and test fixtures) work without a
234	/// `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`.
235	pub fn resolve_repo(
236	    cfg: &UserConfig,
237	    account_override: Option<&str>,
238	    repo_cli: Option<&RepoSelector>,
239	) -> Result<(String, String), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
240	    // Fast path: `--repo <cat>=<val>` is fully self-contained.
241	    if let Some(sel) = repo_cli
242	        && let Some(val) = sel.value.as_ref()
243	    {
244	        debug!(
245	            "resolve_repo: short-circuit — explicit cat={} val={} (from --repo CLI)",
246	            sel.category, val
247	        );
248	        return Ok((sel.category.clone(), val.clone()));
249	    }
250	
251	    // Step 1: pick the AccountConfig to use.
252	    //
253	    // - `--account` CLI → must hit `cfg.accounts[name]`.
254	    // - `[default].account` → must hit `cfg.accounts[that]`.
255	    // - Otherwise fall back to top-level `[repo]` (no account).
256	    // - Otherwise error.
257	    //
258	    // `account_label` is for error messages — section-name "<name>"
259	    // when an account was selected, "<top-level>" for the no-account
260	    // shorthand path.
261	    let (account, account_label, account_source) = match (account_override, &cfg.default_account) {
262	        (Some(name), _) => {
263	            let acct = cfg.accounts.get(name).ok_or_else(|| {
264	                format!("account '{name}' not found in config (no [account.{name}] section)")
265	            })?;
266	            (acct, name.to_string(), "--account CLI")
267	        }
268	        (None, Some(name)) => {
269	            let acct = cfg.accounts.get(name).ok_or_else(|| {
270	                format!(
271	                    "account '{name}' (from [default].account) not found in config \
272	                     (no [account.{name}] section)"
273	                )
274	            })?;
275	            (acct, name.clone(), "[default].account")
276	        }
277	        (None, None) => match cfg.top_level_repo.as_ref() {
278	            Some(r) => (r, String::from("<top-level>"), "top-level [repo]"),
279	            None => {
280	                return Err("no account specified; set [default].account, use \
281	                     --account <name>, or write a top-level [repo] section"
282	                    .into());
283	            }
284	        },
285	    };
286	    debug!(
287	        "resolve_repo: account = {} (from {})",
288	        account_label, account_source
289	    );
290	
291	    // Step 2: category.
292	    let (category, category_source) = match repo_cli {
293	        Some(sel) => (sel.category.clone(), "--repo CLI".to_string()),
294	        None => {
295	            let cat = account.repo_default.clone().ok_or_else(|| {
296	                section_error(
297	                    &account_label,
298	                    "no default category",
299	                    "repo",
300	                    "default",
301	                    "use --repo <cat>",
302	                )
303	            })?;
304	            (cat, format!("[account.{account_label}.repo].default"))
305	        }
306	    };
307	    debug!(
308	        "resolve_repo: category = {} (from {})",
309	        category, category_source
310	    );
311	
312	    // Step 3: value.
313	    let (value, value_source) = match repo_cli.and_then(|s| s.value.clone()) {
314	        Some(v) => (v, "--repo CLI value".to_string()),
315	        None => {
316	            let v = account
317	                .repo_category
318	                .get(&category)
319	                .cloned()
320	                .ok_or_else(|| {
321	                    section_error(
322	                        &account_label,
323	                        &format!("no value for --repo {category}"),
324	                        "repo.category",
325	                        &category,
326	                        &format!("--repo {category}=<val>"),
327	                    )
328	                })?;
329	            (
330	                v,
331	                format!("[account.{account_label}.repo.category].{category}"),
332	            )
333	        }
334	    };
335	    debug!("resolve_repo: value = {} (from {})", value, value_source);
336	
337	    Ok((category, value))
338	}
339	
340	/// Compose an error message for a missing-key case in `resolve_repo`.
341	///
342	/// Renders as `[account.<label>.<section_subpath>].<leaf>` for
343	/// named accounts and `[<section_subpath>].<leaf>` for the
344	/// top-level `[repo]` shorthand.
345	fn section_error(
346	    account_label: &str,
347	    msg: &str,
348	    section_subpath: &str,
349	    leaf: &str,
350	    cli_alt: &str,
351	) -> String {
352	    let section = if account_label == "<top-level>" {
353	        format!("[{section_subpath}]")
354	    } else {
355	        format!("[account.{account_label}.{section_subpath}]")
356	    };
357	    format!("{msg}; set {section}.{leaf} or use {cli_alt}")
358	}
359	
360	#[cfg(test)]
361	mod tests {
362	    use super::*;
363	    use std::path::Path;
364	    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
365	    use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
366	    use std::{fs, path::PathBuf};
367	
368	    use crate::test_tmp_root::{resolve_tmp_root, should_keep_tempdir};
369	
370	    /// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions
371	    /// yield distinct paths when tests run in parallel.
372	    static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
373	
374	    /// RAII tempdir for `config` tests — sibling of `Fixture` /
375	    /// `FixturePor` / `CliFixture`, scaled to the much smaller
376	    /// "write a config.toml and parse it" use case. Replaces the
377	    /// pre-0.41.1-6.4 `cfg_tempdir(tag) -> PathBuf` helper, which
378	    /// had no `Drop` and leaked one dir per test invocation.
379	    ///
380	    /// Uses the shared `resolve_tmp_root` (`$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` →
381	    /// `std::env::temp_dir()`) and honors `should_keep_tempdir()`
382	    /// (`$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`) so the same env-var knobs control all
383	    /// fixture types uniformly.
384	    struct CfgTempDir {
385	        dir: PathBuf,
386	    }
387	
388	    impl CfgTempDir {
389	        fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
390	            let ts = SystemTime::now()
391	                .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
392	                .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
393	                .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming
394	            let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
395	            let dir = resolve_tmp_root().join(format!("vc-x1-cfg-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"));
396	            fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("mkdir cfg tempdir");
397	            Self { dir }
398	        }
399	
400	        fn dir(&self) -> &Path {
401	            &self.dir
402	        }
403	    }
404	
405	    impl Drop for CfgTempDir {
406	        fn drop(&mut self) {
407	            if should_keep_tempdir() {
408	                eprintln!("VC_X1_TEST_KEEP set; preserving {}", self.dir.display());
409	            } else {
410	                let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir);
411	            }
412	        }
413	    }
414	
415	    /// Write `contents` to `<dir>/config.toml`. Returns the
416	    /// `CfgTempDir` guard alongside the file path so callers can
417	    /// keep the guard alive for the test's scope.
418	    fn write_cfg(tag: &str, contents: &str) -> (CfgTempDir, PathBuf) {
419	        let cfg = CfgTempDir::new(tag);
420	        let path = cfg.dir().join("config.toml");
421	        fs::write(&path, contents).unwrap();
422	        (cfg, path)
423	    }
424	
425	    /// `Drop` removes the tempdir on success: keep the path
426	    /// outside the inner scope, drop the guard, and verify the
427	    /// directory is gone. Pins the leak fix that landed in
428	    /// 0.41.1-6.4.
429	    #[test]
430	    fn cfg_tempdir_drop_cleans_up() {
431	        let path: PathBuf;
432	        {
433	            let cfg = CfgTempDir::new("drop-cleanup");
434	            path = cfg.dir().to_path_buf();
435	            assert!(path.exists(), "tempdir must exist while guard is alive");
436	        }
437	        assert!(
438	            !path.exists(),
439	            "tempdir must be removed after CfgTempDir drops, got: {}",
440	            path.display()
441	        );
442	    }
443	
444	    #[test]
445	    fn missing_file_returns_default() {
446	        let cfg = CfgTempDir::new("missing");
447	        let path = cfg.dir().join("nonexistent.toml");
448	        let parsed = load_from(&path).unwrap();
449	        assert_eq!(parsed, UserConfig::default());
450	    }
451	
452	    #[test]
453	    fn empty_file_returns_default() {
454	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg("empty", "");
455	        let cfg = load_from(&path).unwrap();
456	        assert_eq!(cfg, UserConfig::default());
457	    }
458	
459	    #[test]
460	    fn full_schema_parses() {
461	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg(
462	            "full",
463	            r#"[default]
464	account = "home"
465	debug   = "trace"
466	
467	[account.home]
468	repo.default          = "remote"
469	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:winksaville"
470	repo.category.local   = "~/test-fixtures"
471	
472	[account.work]
473	repo.default          = "remote"
474	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:anthropic"
475	repo.category.local   = "/work/fixtures"
476	"#,
477	        );
478	        let cfg = load_from(&path).unwrap();
479	        assert_eq!(cfg.default_account.as_deref(), Some("home"));
480	        assert_eq!(cfg.default_debug.as_deref(), Some("trace"));
481	        assert_eq!(cfg.accounts.len(), 2);
482	
483	        let home = cfg.accounts.get("home").expect("home account");
484	        assert_eq!(home.repo_default.as_deref(), Some("remote"));
485	        assert_eq!(
486	            home.repo_category.get("remote").map(String::as_str),
487	            Some("git@github.com:winksaville")
488	        );
489	        assert_eq!(
490	            home.repo_category.get("local").map(String::as_str),
491	            Some("~/test-fixtures")
492	        );
493	
494	        let work = cfg.accounts.get("work").expect("work account");
495	        assert_eq!(work.repo_default.as_deref(), Some("remote"));
496	        assert_eq!(
497	            work.repo_category.get("remote").map(String::as_str),
498	            Some("git@github.com:anthropic")
499	        );
500	    }

503	    fn account_only_no_default_section() {
504	        // No [default]; one account. accounts populated, top-level None.
505	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg(
506	            "no-default",

508	repo.default          = "remote"

513	        assert!(cfg.default_account.is_none());

520	    fn top_level_repo_shorthand() {
521	        // Single-account shorthand — top-level [repo], no [account.*].
522	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg(
523	            "top-level",

526	category.remote  = "git@github.com:wink"

531	        assert!(cfg.accounts.is_empty());
532	        let tl = cfg.top_level_repo.expect("top_level_repo populated");
533	        assert_eq!(tl.repo_default.as_deref(), Some("remote"));

540	            Some("~/test-fixtures")

545	    fn mixing_top_level_and_accounts_errors() {

547	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg(

561	    fn unknown_keys_ignored() {
562	        // Forward-compat: unknown sections/sub-keys silently dropped.
563	        let (_cfg, path) = write_cfg(
564	            "unknown",

566	repo.category.remote = "git@github.com:wink"

568	repo.unknown-subkey  = "ignored"

570	some-key = "ignored"

574	        let home = cfg.accounts.get("home").unwrap();

580	    fn cfg_with_two_accounts() -> UserConfig {
581	        let home = AccountConfig {
582	            repo_default: Some("remote".into()),
583	            repo_category: HashMap::from([
584	                ("remote".into(), "git@github.com:winksaville".into()),
585	                ("local".into(), "~/test-fixtures".into()),
586	            ]),
587	        };
588	        let work = AccountConfig {
589	            repo_default: Some("remote".into()),
590	            repo_category: HashMap::from([
591	                ("remote".into(), "git@github.com:anthropic".into()),
592	                ("local".into(), "/work/fixtures".into()),
593	            ]),
594	        };
595	        UserConfig {
596	            default_account: Some("home".into()),
597	            default_debug: None,
598	            top_level_repo: None,
599	            accounts: HashMap::from([("home".into(), home), ("work".into(), work)]),
600	        }
601	    }

607	        assert_eq!(cat, "remote");

664	        // `--repo cat=val` is fully self-contained — works even with
665	        // an empty config (no account, no top-level repo).
666	        let cfg = UserConfig::default();
667	        let sel = RepoSelector {

676	    #[test]
677	    fn resolve_explicit_value_ignores_missing_account() {
678	        // Even with --account naming a missing account, the explicit
679	        // --repo cat=val short-circuits without consulting accounts.
680	        let cfg = UserConfig::default();
681	        let sel = RepoSelector {
682	            category: "remote".into(),
683	            value: Some("git@github.com:foo".into()),
684	        };
685	        let (cat, val) = resolve_repo(&cfg, Some("nope"), Some(&sel)).unwrap();
686	        assert_eq!(cat, "remote");
687	        assert_eq!(val, "git@github.com:foo");
688	    }

> AGENT

A few observations before agreeing or disagreeing:

**User config already exists** — `src/config.rs` loads `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` (XDG-aware), with `[default].account`, multi-account `[account.<name>]`, and a top-level `[repo]` shorthand. `vc-x1 init --account <name>` and `--repo <cat>[=<val>]` already resolve through it. So "let's start with `~/.config/vc-config.toml`" is — the file's there; what's missing is a **topology default** key (e.g. `[default].topology = "por"` so `--por` doesn't have to be re-typed).

**My concern with starting there:** that's a *defaults* change, not a *parity* change. T5 parity says "por and dual are first-class equals, same code paths, no afterthought." Adding a config knob that lets users default to por doesn't fix the asymmetry — it just makes the asymmetric path easier to land on. If por is still bolted on, defaulting to it hides bugs behind a config flag.

**Suggested order:**

1. **Parity audit first** — read every subcommand and flag through both lenses (por vs dual). Where does dual get a privileged code path (init's `.vc-config.toml` write, push's `ochid:`, sync's two-repo dance, etc.)? Where is por an afterthought? Produce a list — that's the actual work of T5.
2. **Equalize** — refactor so both topologies route through one shape with a topology parameter, not two near-parallel implementations.
3. **Then** add the topology-default to user config — a small follow-on once both paths are equally good to land on.

Tradeoff: starting with the audit feels slower (no shipping code on day one), but it's the only way to know whether a config default would help users or just funnel them onto a still-broken por path.

Want me to do the parity audit as the `0.61.0-0` Preparation step?

```
1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	## Cycles
4	
5	A cycle has three phases:
6	
7	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
8	  first commit. Sets up the cycle:
9	  - Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.
10	  - Bump `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
11	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically via `## Priorities`)
12	    into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
13	    statement + plan ladder).
14	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
15	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
16	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
17	  needs; each runs through the
18	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
19	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
20	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
21	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
22	    `## Done`.
23	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
24	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
25	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
26	    changed.
27	
28	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
29	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
30	result must always be published at close-out. See
31	[Pushing](#pushing).
32	
33	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
34	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
35	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
36	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
37	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
38	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
39	for the local-ladder mechanics.
40	
41	## Chores sections
42	
43	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
44	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording one cycle:
45	
46	- One section per cycle is the default.
47	- Per-commit sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a
48	  deliberate alternative.
49	
50	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
51	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
52	with the cycle's anticipated close-out title (e.g.
53	`## refactor: foo bar (X.Y.Z)`). The body is empty;
54	content arrives at close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out)).
55	
56	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
57	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
58	CLAUDE.md [Chores conventions](../CLAUDE.md#chores-conventions).
59	
60	## Preparation
61	
62	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
63	
64	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores section
65	  `Commits:` ref** — see
66	  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
67	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
68	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one).
69	  Move into `## In Progress` as:
70	  - A **bold title line** — exact match of the chores
71	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
72	  - A **succinct problem statement**.
73	  - A **plan ladder**.
74	
75	  See [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form). A Todo that
76	  duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process
77	  bug.
78	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
79	  append a `##` header with the cycle's anticipated
80	  close-out title.
81	
82	## Work-N
83	
84	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
85	implement the change. As many as needed:
86	
87	- Each commit runs through the
88	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
89	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
90	  visibility).
91	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
92	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
93	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
94	  scope grows enough (see
95	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
96	
97	## Close-out
98	
99	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
100	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
101	what happens post-squash:
102	
103	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
104	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
105	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
106	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
107	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
108	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
109	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
110	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
111	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
112	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
113	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
114	    back-reference.
115	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
116	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
117	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
118	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
119	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
120	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
121	
122	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
123	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
124	see [Pushing](#pushing).
125	
126	## Numbering
127	
128	The version suffix on each commit encodes its phase —
129	the **final identifier `0` marks a Preparation**:
130	
131	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
132	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
133	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
134	
135	Disambiguation:
136	
137	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
138	  Preparation.
139	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
140	  identifier `0`).
141	
142	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
143	
144	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
145	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
146	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
147	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
148	
149	Sub-cycles needing no Preparation omit `.0` (`-3.1`,
150	`-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later adds
151	`-3.0` without renumbering siblings.
152	
153	Bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase so `vc-x1 -V`
154	reports the active phase at build time.
155	
156	## Per-commit flow
157	
158	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
159	through:
160	
161	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
162	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
163	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
164	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
165	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
166	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
167	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
168	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
169	   1. `cargo fmt`
170	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
171	   3. `cargo test`
172	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
173	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
174	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
175	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
176	   changes and we iterate until complete.
177	6. **Write the commit description** — see
178	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
179	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
180	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
181	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
182	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
183	   keeps the verb visible):
184	
185	   ```
186	   jj commit -m \
187	   "<type>: <short description> (<version>)" \
188	   -m "<intro paragraph>
189	
190	   - file1: gist
191	   - file2: gist
192	
193	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
194	   -R .
195	   ```
196	
197	**Two overrides apply:**
198	
199	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
200	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
201	  surface it; don't push through.
202	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
203	  a review or question forward.
204	
205	## Commit description
206	
207	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/)
208	with a version suffix:
209	
210	```
211	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
212	```
213	
214	### Title
215	
216	- ≤50 chars total (version suffix counts).
217	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
218	  `docs`, `chore`.
219	- Favor terse phrasings.
220	
221	### Body
222	
223	[Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
224	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
225	
226	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
227	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
228	  files with multiple distinct changes:
229	
230	  ```
231	  - path/to/file1
232	    - first distinct change
233	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
234	       with continuation indented 5
235	  ```
236	
237	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
238	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
239	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
240	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
241	
242	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
243	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
244	
245	### Trailer
246	
247	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
248	[ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
249	
250	## Reviewing changes
251	
252	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
253	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
254	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
255	
256	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
257	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
258	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
259	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
260	
261	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
262	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
263	`jj show -r @-`.
264	
265	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
266	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
267	are in [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).
268	
269	## Pushing
270	
271	### Policy
272	
273	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
274	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
275	the cycle's result must be published.
276	
277	### Shape at close-out push
278	
279	Decide the shape and get user approval before pushing —
280	the choice is hard to undo once on the target. Surface
281	the options, wait for the user.
282	
283	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
284	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
285	  focused on one or two files.
286	- **Merge non-ff** — `main` gains a merge commit
287	  (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via two
288	  parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
289	  shape. Set up: `jj rebase -r <tip> --onto <prev>
290	  --onto <sub-tip>` where `<prev>` is the previous
291	  cycle's closed-out commit (current main tip). Two
292	  `--onto` → `<tip>` becomes a merge of `<prev>` +
293	  `<sub-tip>`.
294	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
295	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
296	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
297	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
298	
299	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
300	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
301	
302	### vc-x1 push wrapper
303	
304	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
305	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
306	
307	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
308	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
309	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
310	planned:
311	
312	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
313	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
314	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
315	
316	### .claude cadence
317	
318	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
319	with every code commit in that push.
320	
321	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
322	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
323	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
324	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
325	
326	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
327	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
328	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
329	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
330	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
331	
332	### Bot communication at the reviews
333	
334	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
335	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
336	
337	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
338	  "Work complete. Please review."
339	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
340	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
341	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
342	  --body ...` invocation by default.
343	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
344	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
345	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
346	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
347	
348	### After finalize: stop and wait
349	
350	After `vc-x1 finalize` launches (mid-session per-push or
351	at session-end), you **MUST NEVER** proceed (next step,
352	edit, tool call, text output) until the user explicitly
353	directs you to continue. **Even when the next step seems
354	obvious — wait.** Treat finalize as a hard stop for the
355	whole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
356	*before* executing finalize. Auto-proceeding bypasses
357	the push+finalize checkpoint the user controls.
358	
359	### Recovery
360	
361	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
362	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
363	  finalize by hand:
364	
365	  ```
366	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
367	  ```
368	
369	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
370	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
371	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
372	  stale stage.
373	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
374	  (e.g. updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj
375	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
376	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
377	  push.
378	
379	## ochid trailers
380	
381	A **chid** is jj's change ID — a permanent identifier
382	that survives rebases and describes (see
383	[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md)). An **ochid** trailer
384	on a commit body links it to its counterpart in the
385	other repo via that counterpart's chid, written as a
386	workspace-root-relative path. See
387	[`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) for the
388	multi-line trailer design (multi-user / forking).
389	
390	- **Code-side commits** each carry one
391	  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
392	  change ID.
393	- **The `.claude` commit** has one `ochid: /<code-chid>`
394	  per code commit in that push. More than one occurs on
395	  Merge non-ff close-out (one ochid per Work commit in
396	  the cycle).
397	
398	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
399	IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
400	
401	## Iterative work
402	
403	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
404	from incremental review, loop:
405	
406	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
407	2. Make the next round of changes.
408	3. User reviews the round (see
409	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
410	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
411	   new empty `@`.
412	5. If not done, go to step 2.
413	
414	Same jj mechanics as a
415	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
416	single-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix
417	doesn't change.
418	
419	## Sub-cycle ladders
420	
421	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
422	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
423	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
424	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
425	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
426	for review and bisection only.
427	
428	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
429	
430	For each Work commit in the ladder:
431	
432	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
433	2. Do the commit's work.
434	3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable** — build
435	   and clippy alone miss regressions until a later
436	   commit runs the full suite, raising bisection cost.
437	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
438	   only (no version suffix); the sub-cycle Close-out
439	   collects everything into one final commit with the
440	   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker.
441	
442	### Navigating the ladder
443	
444	Common moves:
445	
446	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
447	  from its base.
448	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
449	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
450	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
451	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
452	  isolation.
453	
454	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
455	descendants auto-rebase.
456	
457	### Close-out: squash the ladder
458	
459	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
460	
461	```
462	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
463	```
464	
465	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
466	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
467	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
468	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
469	
470	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
471	
472	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
473	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
474	
475	### Recovery
476	
477	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
478	prior commits:
479	
480	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
481	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
482	  parent.
483	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
484	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
485	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
486	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
487	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
488	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
489	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
490	
491	# References
492	
493	- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives
494	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
495	- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid
496	  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).
497	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
498	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
499	  for squash-recipe experiments.
500	- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The
501	  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there
502	  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't
503	  caught until a later one ran the full suite.
504	
```

The audit's output is\ninput for future equalization cycles; no code changes\nland in 0.61.0 itself.\n\nThe decision to audit first (vs. starting from a user-config\ntopology default) is captured in the\n[problem statement](../todo.md#in-progress): a defaults\nknob in front of asymmetric paths funnels users onto a\nstill-bolted-on `por` path; equalize first, default later.\n\n### Audit scope\n\nAreas the audit walk inspects, each producing its own\nsection in `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md`:\n\n- **`options_flags/por.rs`** — the `--por` gate itself;\n  how it's threaded through commands.\n- **`init`** — `.vc-config.toml` write, `.claude/`\n  companion creation, template seeding, push retries.\n- **`clone`** — mirror of init for an existing remote.\n- **`push`** — two-repo coordination, `ochid:`\n  composition, finalize handoff.\n- **`sync`** — two-repo pull dance, `-R` flag wiring.\n- **`finalize`** — `.claude` cadence (one push = one\n  `.claude` commit), squash + push detach.\n- **`show` / `chid` / `desc` helpers / `validate-desc`\n  / `fix-desc`** — anything that reads or writes ochid\n  trailers or `.claude` state.\n- **Tests and fixtures** — where `FixturePor` and the\n  dual fixture diverge in coverage.\n\nThe audit doesn't propose fixes inline; it produces a\nparity-gap table that close-out turns into ranked\nfollow-up Todo entries."

Audit doc written at `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md` (snapshot of `463e6bd9`, read-only). Eight sections + parity-gap summary table.

Headline findings:

- **`push` is the biggest gap** — entirely dual-baked. No `--por` flag on `PushArgs`; the state machine names `CommitClaude` / `FinalizeClaude` stages; `claude_path()` hard-codes `<root>/.claude`; commit bodies stamp `ochid: /.claude/...` / `ochid: /...` unconditionally. Running `vc-x1 push` against a por workspace would resolve nonexistent `.claude/...` paths.
- **`PorFlag` reaches only `init` and `clone`** — every other subcommand infers topology indirectly from `.vc-config.toml`. Creation-time flag, no runtime equivalent.
- **`validate-desc` / `fix-desc` are dual-required** — `other_repo_from_config` errors with "missing workspace.other-repo" on por; no topology check, no skip-when-por path.
- **`sync` is the parity model already** — `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` (in `common.rs:569-653`) handle por cleanly: missing workspace root or empty `other-repo` → `Scope([Code])` → cwd.
- **`init` / `clone` defaulting is asymmetric** — dual is the default branch; `--por` is the opt-out; `--config` is only valid with `--por`.
- **Tests:** `FixturePor` exists but only `init/tests.rs` uses it (5x); all `push` and `sync` integration tests use the dual `Fixture` (8 + 9 uses).
- **`finalize` body is topology-neutral** — single-repo operator; only the surrounding `push` workflow makes it dual-shaped.

No areas where I got stuck; the codebase is small enough that grep + file reads covered everything cleanly.

Work complete. Please review.

Summary of `-1` audit walk:

- **`notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md`** (new, 284 lines) — eight `##` sections (one per audit area) + Summary table. Snapshot pinned to commit `463e6bd9` (0.61.0-0).
- **`Cargo.toml`** — version bump to `0.61.0-1`.
- **`notes/todo.md`** — `-1` ladder step flipped to `(done)`.

**Headline findings from the audit** (full details in the doc):

- **`push` is the largest gap** (Severity High, Eq size L) — entirely dual-baked; no `--por` on `PushArgs`; `claude_path()` hard-codes `<root>/.claude`; commit bodies stamp `ochid: /.claude/...` unconditionally; running it against a por workspace would resolve nonexistent paths.
- **`PorFlag` reach is limited** to `init` + `clone` (High, L) — every other subcommand infers topology indirectly from `.vc-config.toml`. Creation-time flag, no runtime equivalent.
- **`validate-desc` / `fix-desc` are dual-required** (Medium, M) — `other_repo_from_config` errors with "missing workspace.other-repo" on por.
- **`sync` is the parity model already** (Low, S) — `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` in `common.rs:569-653` handle por cleanly via `Scope([Code])`. Closest existing template for "topology from config, not from a flag."
- **`init` / `clone`** symmetric at the orchestrator layer but asymmetric at the defaulting layer (dual default; `--por` opt-out; `--config` is por-only).
- **`finalize` body is topology-neutral** — single-repo operator; only its surrounding `push` workflow makes it dual-shaped.
- **Tests** — `FixturePor` exists but only `init/tests.rs` uses it (5x); all `push` (8x) and `sync` (9x) integration tests use the dual fixture.

Cargo cycle: `cargo install` only (docs-only commit, no Rust changed).

Ready to commit when you've reviewed.

This'll be -1 great, but rather than closing-out next let's make -2 an analysis and see what's common and what's por|dual only and then see if we codify in -3 or close-out. Thoughts?

> AGENT

Yes — `-2` analysis is a good call. The `-1` doc catalogs *divergences*; an analysis pass adds the inverse view (what's already shared) and is exactly the input you'd need before any equalization design.

**What I'd put in `-2`:**

A per-subcommand commonality view — for each of init/clone/push/sync/finalize/ochid-helpers/tests, three buckets:

- **Shared** — code that runs the same on both topologies today (e.g. `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` in `common.rs`, the bookmark/push retry logic, `finalize`'s repo-agnostic body).
- **Dual-only** — code that assumes `.claude/` exists (e.g. `claude_path()`, ochid composition, the dual `Fixture`).
- **Por-only** — anything gated behind `--por` that dual skips (e.g. `init`'s `--config` flag, single-remote setup).

Plus one summary: which subcommands are mostly-shared with a thin dual veneer (good equalization candidates) vs. structurally dual (`push`).

**On `-3` codify:** I'd lean toward close-out, not `-3`. Codifying a design pattern (topology trait? scope-everywhere? config-driven dispatch?) in CLAUDE.md *before* prototyping it on a small target (e.g. fixing `validate-desc` to no-op on por) risks ossifying a shape that doesn't survive first contact with code. The bot thinks the right "codification" is a small follow-up equalization cycle in 0.62.0, not a CLAUDE.md rule written from analysis alone.

So my suggested updated ladder:
- `-0` Prep (done)
- `-1` Audit walk (done)
- `-2` Commonality analysis — produces `notes/por-dual-parity-analysis.md` (or appends a `## Commonality` section to the audit doc)
- Close-out — synthesize audit + analysis into ranked follow-up Todo entries; `-3` codification only if `-2` surfaces something concretely codifiable.

Which file shape do you want for `-2` — sibling analysis doc, or append to the audit doc?

594	/// Resolve the workspace's default `--scope`.
595	///
596	/// Reads `<workspace_root>/.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`:
597	///
598	/// - dual workspace (non-empty `other-repo`) → `Scope([Code, Bot])`
599	/// - single-repo workspace (missing / empty `other-repo`) →
600	///   `Scope([Code])`
601	/// - POR (no `workspace_root`) → `Scope([Code])` — `scope_to_repos`
602	///   resolves `Side::Code` to cwd's `.` when no root is given, so a
603	///   POR run still operates on the current directory.
604	pub fn default_scope(workspace_root: Option<&Path>) -> Scope {
605	    let Some(root) = workspace_root else {
606	        return Scope(vec![Side::Code]);
607	    };
608	    let cfg = match toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE)) {
609	        Ok(c) => c,
610	        Err(_) => return Scope(vec![Side::Code]),
611	    };
612	    match toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo") {
613	        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
614	        _ => Scope(vec![Side::Code]),
615	    }
616	}
617	
618	/// Resolve a `Scope` to concrete repo paths.
619	///
620	/// - `Side::Code` → `workspace_root` (or cwd's `.` when
621	///   `workspace_root` is None).
622	/// - `Side::Bot` → `workspace_root.join(other-repo)`.
623	///
624	/// Errors when `Side::Bot` is requested but the workspace doesn't
625	/// define one (POR or single-repo workspace).
626	pub fn scope_to_repos(
627	    scope: &Scope,
628	    workspace_root: Option<&Path>,
629	) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
630	    let mut repos = Vec::new();
631	    for side in &scope.0 {
632	        match side {
633	            Side::Code => repos.push(
634	                workspace_root
635	                    .map(Path::to_path_buf)
636	                    .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(".")),
637	            ),
638	            Side::Bot => {
639	                let root = workspace_root.ok_or(
640	                    "--scope=bot: not in a vc-x1 workspace (no .vc-config.toml with path = \"/\") — drop --scope or use --scope=code",
641	                )?;
642	                let cfg = toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
643	                let other = toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo")
644	                    .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
645	                    .ok_or(
646	                        "--scope=bot: no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = \"…\"` to .vc-config.toml to enable dual-repo operations",
647	                    )?;
648	                repos.push(root.join(other));
649	            }
650	        }
651	    }
652	    Ok(repos)
653	}
654	
655	/// Resolve `CommonArgs.repo` + `CommonArgs.scope` into concrete repo paths.
656	///
657	/// The four `CommonArgs` consumers (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`)
658	/// call this at the start of their body to translate the clap surface
659	/// into the `&[PathBuf]` that `for_each_repo` iterates. Behavior matches
660	/// today's defaults plus a composing rule for the new `--scope` flag:
661	///
662	/// - neither flag → `[.]` (today's no-arg default).
663	/// - `-R PATH` alone → `[PATH]` (today's `-R <path>` behavior;

The shared\nbody doesn't need changes.\n\n#### `init` / `clone` — shared body, dual default\n\n- **Shared** — repo-creation primitives (`init_one` /\n  `clone_one` per `chores-07.md > init + clone redesign\n  (0.41.1)`), GitHub remote provisioning, push retries,\n  template seeding mechanism, `--account` / `--repo`\n  resolution via `config.rs`.\n- **Dual-only** — the orchestration outer loop runs twice\n  (code + bot) when `--por` is absent; `.vc-config.toml`\n  write; `.claude/` directory creation; `--use-template\n  <code,bot>` accepts a bot value.\n- **Por-only** — `--config <none|PATH>` (overrides the\n  canned `.vc-config.toml` write, only meaningful when\n  there'd be one).\n\nThe asymmetry is at the *defaulting* layer (dual is the\nimplicit default; `--por` is the opt-out) more than the\n*code* layer. The body is already roughly symmetric.\n\n#### `push` — dual-only\n\n- **Shared** — bookmark tracking, the push state machine's\n  generic stages (`prepare`, `commit-app`, `push-app`),\n  the retry/resume scaffolding around `.vc-x1/push-state.toml`.\n- **Dual-only** — `claude_path()` resolution; the\n  `CommitClaude` / `FinalizeClaude` stages; ochid trailer\n  composition (`ochid: /.claude/<chid>` on app, multi-line\n  `ochid: /<code-chid>` on bot); the `--from\n  bookmark-both` flag; the 1:1 symmetric WC-commits\n  assumption flagged in T1.\n- **Por-only** — none.\n\nThe largest gap. The bot thinks no por code path exists\n*at all* today; a por workspace running `vc-x1 push` would\nresolve nonexistent `.claude/` paths during the\n`CommitClaude` stage.\n\n#### `finalize` — body shared, use site dual\n\n- **Shared (all)** — the body is a single-repo operator\n  (squash + push + cleanup). Topology-neutral.\n- **Dual-only** — none in the body. The dual shape is\n  *external*: `push` schedules a detached `finalize`\n  against `.claude` only after `push-app` succeeds.\n- **Por-only** — none.\n\nOnce `push` gains a por path, `finalize` requires no\nchanges.\n\n#### Tests and fixtures\n\n- **Shared** — `Fixture` / `CliFixture` provide a dual\n  workspace; `FixturePor` provides a por workspace; both\n  honor `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` / `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` and the\n  RAII drop pattern.\n- **Dual-only** — every integration test for `push` (8\n  uses) and `sync` (9 uses); the `chid` / `desc` / `show` /\n  `list` test paths (these route through `CommonArgs`,\n  which works on por, but the fixtures don't cover that\n  case).\n- **Por-only** — five uses in `init/tests.rs` exercising\n  the `--por` initialization path.\n\nThe bot thinks the test-coverage gap mirrors the runtime\ngap exactly: `push` and the desc outliers have zero por\ncoverage because the runtime code doesn't support por\nthere; the `CommonArgs` family has zero por coverage\ndespite supporting it.\n\n### Equalization candidates, ranked\n\nOrdered from closest-to-shared (cheap wins) to\nfurthest (architectural work):\n\n1. **`validate-desc` / `fix-desc`** — local refactor:\n   replace the `other_repo_from_config` prelude with a\n   scope-aware resolution that no-ops when `Side::Bot`\n   is absent. Body unchanged. The bot thinks this is the\n   smallest concrete equalization and a good prototype.\n2. **`CommonArgs`-family por test coverage** — pure test\n   work; surface bugs in scope handling without\n   architectural risk. Likely lands a `FixturePor` use\n   in `chid`/`desc`/`show`/`list` test modules.\n3. **`init` / `clone` defaulting** — make `--por` /\n   `--dual` peer flags rather than dual-default + `--por`\n   opt-out; thread the chosen topology through the body\n   without changing the inner primitives. User-config\n   `[default].topology` (the original user proposal)\n   becomes a small follow-on once peers exist.\n4. **`push`** — the structural one. `claude_path()`,\n   stage names, ochid composition all carry the\n   dual-shape baked in. The bot thinks this is best done\n   *after* `--por` becomes a runtime-known fact across\n   all subcommands (i.e. after the desc outliers and\n   defaulting are handled), so push has a stable contract\n   to dispatch against.\n5. **Topology-from-config rule** — codify (CLAUDE.md or a\n   small ARCHITECTURE note) that every runtime subcommand\n   resolves topology via `default_scope`, never from a\n   `--por` flag. The flag stays creation-time only. The\n   bot thinks this rule is the right *outcome* of the\n   equalization above, not a prerequisite — codify it\n   once the prototype validates the shape.\n\n### Summary\n\nThree structural classes today:\n\n- **Topology-neutral via scope** —\n  `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` / `sync` / `finalize`-body.\n  This is the working pattern.\n- **Topology-required (dual)** —\n  `push` / `validate-desc` / `fix-desc`. These bypass\n  scope and assume `.claude/` exists.\n- **Topology-creating** — `init` / `clone`. The `--por`\n  flag's only legitimate home; chooses the workspace\n  shape that downstream commands then read from\n  `.vc-config.toml`.\n\nEqualization is \"make Topology-required match\nTopology-neutral via scope\" — not a new pattern, just\nextending an existing one. The smallest concrete win is\n`validate-desc` / `fix-desc`; the largest is `push`. The\ntwo creation-time commands stay roughly as-is, with the\ndefaulting layer (peer flags + optional user-config\ndefault) as the only change.\n\n# References"

> AGENT

Edits done, not committed. Ready to talk.

**`-2` adds**: `## Commonality` section in `por-dual-parity-audit.md` (+181 lines, doc now 464 total). Cargo bumped to `0.61.0-2`. Ladder in `## In Progress` updated (3→4 commits) and `-2` marked `(done)`.

The Commonality section has three layers:

1. **Per-subcommand bucket lists** — for chid/desc/list/show, sync, validate-desc/fix-desc, init/clone, push, finalize, tests/fixtures — what's shared, dual-only, por-only.
2. **Ranked equalization candidates** (5 items) — cheapest first:
   - validate-desc/fix-desc (local refactor, scope-aware prelude)
   - CommonArgs-family por test coverage
   - init/clone defaulting → peer flags
   - push (the structural one)
   - codify topology-from-config rule (after prototype validates it)
3. **Summary**: three structural classes today — *topology-neutral via scope* (the working pattern), *topology-required dual* (push + desc outliers), *topology-creating* (init/clone). Equalization = "extend the existing pattern," not invent a new one.

**Headline insight that fell out of the analysis**: the codebase already has the right pattern (`default_scope` + `scope_to_repos` + `for_each_repo`) — half the subcommands route through it cleanly. The "dual is bolted on" framing inverts when you look this way: por is bolted on **everywhere the scope pattern wasn't applied**. Push and validate-desc/fix-desc are the holdouts.

What did you want to discuss?

> DEVELOPER

Let's talk about the "goals", for starters right now `vc-x1 init --por por1` creates a ./por1:

wink@3900x 26-05-26T20:11:58.962Z:/tmp/por1 (main+1)


It also creates https://github.com/winksaville/por1

So full parity with "dual", But if you don't want .vc-config.toml or you don't want the remote repo, too bad that's not supported.
So we need to define the "features" we want and what their parity should be.

I think we should do that in -3, thoughts?

> AGENT

Yes, `-3` for feature decomposition is the right call — and your example sharpens what I missed in `-2`. `--por` today is a **tangled bundle**, not an independent axis. The dimensions are conflated:

1. **Topology** — single repo vs. code+`.claude/` pair.
2. **`.vc-config.toml`** — written or not.
3. **Remote provisioning** — create GitHub repo or local-only.
4. **(possible)** — jj init, `.gitignore` template seeding, `.vc-x1/` state dir, etc.

Today: `--por` collapses #1 to single, leaves #2 still happening (just with a degenerate `[workspace] path = "/"`), and leaves #3 unchanged (still hits GitHub). The user has no way to ask for "single repo, no `.vc-config.toml`, no remote" or any of the other independent combinations.

The audit's "T5 parity" framing missed this. Parity isn't just "dual and por treated equally" — it's "the underlying axes are independently controllable, and the names dual/por are *combinations* of those axes, not primitives."

**What `-3` should produce:**

- A `## Feature axes` section in the audit doc (or a new sibling — your call) that:
  - **Names each independent axis** with current state, target state, and proposed flag.
  - **Defines the defaults** (probably: dual topology, config written, remote created — preserving today's no-arg `init` behavior).
  - **Maps `--por` and `--dual` onto axis combinations** for back-compat shorthand.
  - **Identifies what's missing** — no current flag for "no remote," no current flag for "no `.vc-config.toml`" (`--config none` is close but reads as override-not-disable).
  - **Connects to user-config** — once axes are independent, `[default].topology`, `[default].create-remote`, `[default].write-vc-config` are obvious follow-on knobs (and the original user proposal lands cleanly here).

**One caution:** keep `-3` to *defining* the axes, not implementing them. The implementation lands in 0.62.0+ equalization cycles. Otherwise `-3` swells past a docs commit.

**Ladder update:**

- `-0` Prep (done)
- `-1` Audit walk (done)
- `-2` Commonality analysis (done)
- `-3` Feature-axes decomposition — name the independent axes, defaults, mapping from `--por`/`--dual`, gap list
- Close-out — synthesize all three layers (where divergences live × what's already shared × what axes should exist) into ranked follow-up Todo entries

The bot thinks this three-layer view (divergence × commonality × feature-axes) is the right input for 0.62.0 to make concrete equalization choices, where 0.61.0 alone wouldn't have given.

Want me to go ahead with `-3` on those lines?

> DEVELOPER

yes, go ahead with -3

> AGENT

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md",
  "old_string": "defaulting layer (peer flags + optional user-config\ndefault) as the only change.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "defaulting layer (peer flags + optional user-config\ndefault) as the only change.\n\n## Feature axes\n\nThe audit and commonality passes treat `--por` and `--dual`\nas the primitives. They aren't — they're bundles. The\nconcrete shape `vc-x1 init --por <name>` produces today is\n\"single repo, with a (degenerate) `.vc-config.toml`, pushed\nto a freshly-created GitHub repo.\" Each of those is an\nindependent choice, but the user can only ask for them as a\nbundle.\n\nThis section names the independent axes, says what each\ncontrols, lists today's flags and defaults, and identifies\ngaps. **No implementation — defining only.** Equalization\nacross these axes lands in 0.62.0+ cycles.\n\n### Axes\n\n#### A1. Topology\n\n- **States** — `single` (one code repo) | `dual` (code repo\n  + `.claude/` companion cross-linked by `ochid:`).\n- **Today** — dual is the implicit default; `--por`\n  is the boolean opt-out. No `--dual` peer.\n- **Surface** — `--por` (boolean, `init` and `clone` only).\n  Downstream commands infer from `.vc-config.toml >\n  [workspace] other-repo` via `default_scope`.\n- **Gap** — `--dual` doesn't exist as a peer flag, so the\n  defaulting is asymmetric: you can be explicit about por\n  but not about dual.\n\n#### A2. `.vc-config.toml` write\n\n- **States** — `written` (canned content) | `not-written`\n  | `overridden` (caller-supplied path copied in).\n- **Today** — written by default. `--config none|<path>`\n  selects `not-written` or `overridden`, **but is rejected\n  unless `--por` is also set** (`init.rs:610`: \"--config\n  is only valid with --por (dual configs are per-side and\n  unconditional)\").\n- **Surface** — `--config <none|PATH>`.\n- **Gap** — not orthogonal to A1. The dual path forces\n  `written` with canned content; no way to override.\n- **Constraint** — dual `other-repo` resolution reads\n  `.vc-config.toml`; `(A1=dual, A2=not-written)` would\n  break runtime topology detection. So the matrix may\n  legitimately forbid that combination, but the error\n  shape should be \"you asked for an impossible\n  combination,\" not \"this flag is only valid with `--por`.\"\n\n#### A3. Remote provisioning\n\n- **States** — `github-create` (default; creates\n  `winksaville/<name>` via gh CLI) | `github-skip` (URL\n  is non-GitHub or pre-existing) | `local-bare` (`git\n  init --bare` under `--repo local=<dir>`) | `none`\n  (no remote configured at all — just the working repo).\n- **Today** — `--repo <cat>[=<val>]` resolves to one of\n  the first three via the user-config `[account.<a>.\n  repo.category]` lookup; `none` doesn't exist.\n- **Surface** — `--repo` for the cat/val, `--private` for\n  GitHub visibility, `--push-retries` /\n  `--push-retry-delay` for the post-create push.\n- **Gap** — no flag for \"no remote.\" A user wanting a\n  local workspace (or to provision the remote separately)\n  has no clean way to express it.\n\n#### A4. Private vs public\n\n- **States** — `public` (default) | `private`.\n- **Today** — `--private` flag.\n- **Surface** — `--private`.\n- **Scope** — only meaningful when A3 = `github-create`.\n- **Gap** — none structurally; the flag is independent\n  and orthogonal.\n\n#### A5. Template seeding\n\n- **States** — `none` (default) | `code-only <path>` |\n  `code-and-bot <path,path>`.\n- **Today** — `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>`. Second value\n  is dual-only.\n- **Surface** — `--use-template`.\n- **Gap** — none structurally; per-side templating is\n  naturally A1-aware. Bot-side template under A1=single\n  is meaningless and rejected today.\n\n#### A6. Working-tree scaffolding (jj init, `.gitignore`)\n\n- **States** — `on` (default) | `off`.\n- **Today** — always `on`. No flag.\n- **Gap** — probably not worth a knob. jj is the workspace\n  scaffolding; opting out doesn't yield a meaningful\n  workspace. `.gitignore` is unconditional and a fixed\n  content list. The bot thinks A6 stays a non-axis\n  unless a concrete use case surfaces.\n\n### Defaults summary\n\n| Axis | Default | Today's flag | Independent today? |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| A1 Topology | dual | `--por` (opt-out only) | No (no `--dual` peer) |\n| A2 `.vc-config.toml` | written | `--config <none\\|PATH>` | No (rejected when dual) |\n| A3 Remote | github-create | `--repo <cat>[=<val>]` | Mostly (`none` missing) |\n| A4 Private | public | `--private` | Yes |\n| A5 Template | none | `--use-template <C[,B]>` | Yes (A1-aware) |\n| A6 Scaffolding | on | (none) | Not an axis today |\n\n### Mapping `--por` / `--dual` onto axis combinations\n\nToday `--por` and (implicit) `--dual` are *bundle\nshorthands*. After axes are independent, they remain as\nshorthands for the common combinations:\n\n- `--dual` (or no flag — back-compat) → `(A1=dual,\n  A2=written, A3=github-create, A4=public, A5=none)`.\n- `--por` → `(A1=single, A2=written, A3=github-create,\n  A4=public, A5=none)`. Note A2=written is what today's\n  `--por` actually does, not `not-written` (the\n  `.vc-config.toml` is still written, just degenerate).\n\nA user wanting a fully-plain single repo today can't\nspell it; tomorrow it would be `--por --config none --no-remote` (or\nshorthand `--bare` if that combination is common enough to\ndeserve one).\n\n### Connections to user-config\n\nOnce axes are independent, each maps to a `[default].*`\nkey in `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`, so the user-config\nproposal that opened this cycle lands cleanly:\n\n- `[default].topology = \"single\" | \"dual\"`\n- `[default].write-vc-config = true | false | \"<path>\"`\n- `[default].remote = \"github\" | \"local\" | \"none\"`\n- `[default].private = true | false`\n\n`--account`-scoped overrides ride on the existing\n`[account.<a>]` substrate. The bot thinks A3 is the most\nuseful default to make user-configurable (different\naccounts → different remote providers), with A1 second.\n\n### Gap list (input for close-out)\n\nThe concrete gaps for follow-up cycles to seed Todos\nagainst:\n\n1. **A1 has no `--dual` peer** — asymmetric defaulting.\n   Add `--dual` (alias of \"no `--por`\") for explicit\n   parity; allow `--por`/`--dual` exactly one of.\n2. **A2 errors on dual** — change the error to\n   \"(A1=dual, A2=not-written) is impossible because dual\n   needs `.vc-config.toml` for runtime topology\" rather\n   than the current flag-restriction message; allow\n   `--config <path>` (override) under `--dual`.\n3. **A3 missing `none`** — add a way to spell \"no remote.\"\n   Probably `--repo none` (orthogonal to the existing\n   `remote`/`local` categories) or a separate `--no-remote`\n   flag. The bot thinks `--repo none` keeps the\n   one-knob-for-A3 shape.\n4. **A6 is non-negotiable today** — confirm at close-out\n   whether to leave it that way; if so, drop A6 from the\n   axis list.\n5. **User-config keys** — once A1–A5 are independent,\n   wire `[default].topology` / `.write-vc-config` /\n   `.remote` / `.private` into the `init` resolution\n   chain. Follow the three-step `resolve_repo` shape\n   (CLI → `[default]` → error).\n\nThese five become candidate `## Todo` entries at\nclose-out, ranked by the equalization-candidate ordering\nfrom `## Commonality` (axis fixes that overlap with\n`validate-desc` / `fix-desc` equalization land cheapest).\n\n# References"
}
