> AGENT

Strong agreement on the principle, and an observation: **this is already the working pattern in the codebase**. `src/config.rs`'s `resolve_repo` does exactly this for `--account` and `--repo`: CLI → `[default].account` → `[account.<a>.repo].default` → error with the exact key to set. Extending it to A1 (and A3–A5) isn't a new rule, it's *consistency* — the existing precedent just hasn't been applied to topology yet.

**Resolution chain for A1, mirroring `resolve_repo`:**

1. `--por` or `--dual` on the CLI → use that.
2. `[default].topology` in `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` → use that.
3. Otherwise error: `"no topology specified; set [default].topology = \"single\"|\"dual\" or pass --por / --dual"`.

Exactly-one-of `--por`/`--dual` (clap-enforced). Internal value lives as an enum, not a boolean — kills the `--por`/`!--por` framing that the audit found everywhere.

**Why the principle is right across all axes** (not just A1):

- For A1 / A3: the consequences of "wrong default" are real (got a `.claude/` you didn't want; pushed to a GitHub account you didn't mean to). Explicit forces the choice once.
- For A4 (private/public): today's silent default-public is a **security smell**. Making it explicit is a feature, not friction.
- For A5 (template): default = none is so universal it barely counts as a "default" — but the principle still applies cheaply.
- For A2 (`.vc-config.toml` write): once dual *requires* it and single makes it *optional*, the axis collapses to one knob (`--config none|<path>|written`) that's only meaningful under single. Probably resolve A2 *after* A1, with "not specified → written" being the only safe single-side default.

**Two implications worth flagging now:**

- **Test fixtures rewrite.** Every integration test in `init/tests.rs`, `push/tests.rs`, `sync/integration_tests.rs` runs without explicit topology today. Once it's required, all tests must spell it. Tractable — likely a global `--dual` add or a fixture-level default — but real work for the equalization cycle.
- **Bootstrap UX.** New user runs `vc-x1 init foo` cold, gets an error. Error message must be the doc — pointing at the exact key + showing the canned shape. The existing `section_error` helper in `config.rs:345` is the model; reuse it.

**One small disagreement / nuance:**

For A6 (jj init / `.gitignore`) I'd still drop it from the axis list entirely — not "default = on, must be set in config," but "this isn't a meaningful knob at all." `vc-x1 init` without jj is `git init`; `vc-x1 init` without the canned `.gitignore` is the user typing four lines manually. Neither variation is useful enough to maintain. The bot thinks A6 stays non-axis; calling it out as such in the doc is fine.

**Open question I'd push back on:** for A2 specifically, should `[default].write-vc-config` even exist? The constraint matrix says dual *forces* written; single *allows* not-written. So `[default].write-vc-config = false` is silently overridden under dual. That's a footgun — the default says one thing, the chosen topology overrides it. Either:

- (a) Make it strictly under-single (A2 doesn't appear when A1=dual); user-config knob only kicks in when single is chosen.
- (b) Drop the user-config knob entirely; A2 is a per-invocation choice via `--config` only.

The bot leans (a) — keep the knob, but document that it only applies under single. (b) loses the "set it once" benefit you're after.

Want to lock in A1 as I sketched and move to A2?

> DEVELOPER

I tend to agree with you, but other nuances.
1) I specifically want to be able to convert an existing por to a dual
2) When you have a dual and add --por treat it as a por and ignore vc-config.toml (local and global)? Answer: Y
3) Should a por ever use vc-config.toml or create vc-config.toml (local and global)? Answer: N if the above is Y
Thoughts?

> AGENT

Good probing questions. Let me untangle one ambiguity and then react to each.

**Ambiguity first**: "ignore vc-config.toml (local and global)" — there are two distinct files with similar names:

- **Workspace** `.vc-config.toml` at the repo root — written by `init`, defines `[workspace] path = "/"` and `[workspace] other-repo`. Workspace metadata.
- **User-config** at `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` — defines `[default].*`, `[account.<n>].*`. User defaults.

The audit / commonality work has only really touched the *workspace* file. Your "local" must be workspace; "global" is presumably user-config. I'll separate them in my reactions.

---

**(1) `por → dual` conversion** — agreed, that's T4 today. Once por is fully `.vc-config.toml`-free (per #3 below), conversion is clean: it just writes `.vc-config.toml` + creates `.claude/` from scratch. No "upgrade a degenerate config" needed.

**(2) Dual workspace + `--por` flag → treat as por, ignore workspace `.vc-config.toml`** — agreed, with one nuance. This means `--por` becomes a **runtime override**, not just creation-time. Today it appears only on `init`/`clone`; under your rule it'd appear on every subcommand and short-circuit `default_scope` → `Scope([Code])` regardless of what the workspace file says. That's actually useful — escape hatch for "this command, just my code, leave `.claude/` alone." Worth doing.

**(3) Should por ever use or create `.vc-config.toml`?** — agreed: **No** for the *workspace* file. If `--por` runtime-overrides everything, the workspace file's only purpose is dual's `other-repo` lookup, and por never needs that. Today's `vc-x1 init --por foo` writing a degenerate `[workspace] path = "/"` file is residue from when `--por` was a half-implemented opt-out. Drop the write entirely.

---

**One distinction I'd push back on, though**: I'd keep por reading the **user-config** (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`). Otherwise por loses:

- A3 — `[default].account = "home"` so `vc-x1 init --por foo` knows where to push without retyping `--repo remote=...`.
- A4 — `[default].private = true` so a "private-by-default" user gets it on por too.
- A1 — the very rule we just locked in: "if topology is not specified, look in `[default].topology` before erroring." A naked `vc-x1 init foo` user-config-says-"single" should produce por, no flag needed.

The bot thinks the right framing is:

- **Workspace `.vc-config.toml`** — dual-only. Por never reads, never creates. (Your #3 = Y under this reading.)
- **User-config `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`** — topology-neutral. Both por and dual consult it for defaults across all axes.

The two files have different jobs: workspace is *runtime topology detection*, user-config is *user defaults*. Dropping the first for por is correct; dropping the second would gut the "set it once" benefit of user-config that motivated `-3` in the first place.

**Does that distinction match what you meant?** If you meant "por ignores both files" I'd want to hear the case for cutting user-config too — I might be missing something.

> AGENT

Both useful — let me sketch the layered shape they push us toward, then flag scope and one piece I'd cut.

---

**Resolution chain becomes 4 layers (with an explicit-required floor):**

1. **CLI** — `--por`, `--account`, `--config <path>`, `--global-config <path>`, etc.
2. **Env-var** — `VC_X1_<KEY>` overrides config files.
3. **Local config** — `./.vc-config.toml` or `--config <path>`.
4. **Global config** — `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` or `--global-config <path>` (or pointed at by local).
5. **Error** — "no `<key>` specified; here are the 4 surfaces" (the floor we agreed on in A1).

Same shape as today's `resolve_repo`, just with env-var inserted between CLI and config-files, and local added between env-var and global. Each layer is *optional*; you can use any subset.

---

**Reactions to your two asks:**

**(a) `--no-global-config`** — agreed. Necessary for tests / CI / "ignore my defaults this once." For symmetry I'd also add **`--no-local-config`** — but flag that today it would only do what runtime `--por` already does (ignore workspace topology), because local config carries nothing else. Add it as a peer once local config gains non-topology fields; until then it's redundant with `--por`.

**(b) `VC_X1_<KEY>` env-var support** — agreed, with a flat naming convention to mirror the resolved-key namespace:

- `VC_X1_TOPOLOGY` → A1 (`single`/`dual`)
- `VC_X1_ACCOUNT` → A3 account
- `VC_X1_REPO` → A3 category[=value]
- `VC_X1_PRIVATE` → A4 (true/false)
- `VC_X1_USE_TEMPLATE` → A5
- `VC_X1_CONFIG` → path or `none` (overrides local)
- `VC_X1_GLOBAL_CONFIG` → path (overrides global)
- `VC_X1_NO_LOCAL_CONFIG`, `VC_X1_NO_GLOBAL_CONFIG` → boolean

Precedent: `config.rs` already honors `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; tests already use `VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` / `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`. So `VC_X1_*` is established.

---

**Two concerns worth raising before we accept:**

**Scope creep.** `-3` was scoped to "name independent feature axes." We're now scoping a 4-layer config-resolution system, which is **its own design exercise**. The bot thinks two reasonable moves:

- (i) Document this in the existing `## Feature axes` section as the **resolution chain** subsection — concise (the per-axis sections already say "CLI → user-config → error"; this just slots env-var + local + `--no-global-config` into the picture). No code in `-3`; defining only.
- (ii) Spin a sibling note (`notes/config-layering.md`) to keep the audit doc focused, and reference it.

I lean (i) — it's the same shape, just one more layer; the audit doc is the natural home. If it grows past ~50 lines we split.

**One piece I'd cut: "global config path defined in local `.vc-config.toml`."** You mentioned "defined in local .vc-config.toml" as one of the global-config sources. I'd drop that:

- It's a third path to specify the global, on top of `--global-config` and `~/.config/`. Three ways to do one thing.
- It enables "team-wide shared defaults" (local points at a checked-in config), but that use case is served by `--global-config $(cat .vc-config-global-path)` or a project-level shell alias.
- Removing it keeps local and global as **co-equal**, not "local can mutate the global resolution."

If you want the "team-wide shared defaults" capability, the cleaner shape is letting `--global-config` accept a relative path, so a script in the project can do `vc-x1 init --global-config ./team-config.toml ...`. No magic file-content interpretation.

---

**Want me to write up the 4-layer chain into the doc as a new `### Resolution chain` subsection under `## Feature axes`?** That captures everything we just settled and keeps `-3` from creeping further. I'd also note `--no-local-config` as a deferred peer (today redundant with `--por`).

We already are specifying 4 layers and I think local config should be able to override every CLI. Why say local config can override "every CLI except --global-config". Also Global config can specify a different Global config, if it's circular tough shit :)

> AGENT

Got it — let me confirm I'm reading you right before writing it up.

**Your model**: local config has *universal* precedence over CLI (and presumably env-var). No carve-outs. The project's `.vc-config.toml` is sovereign over the project's behavior; CLI is for fields the project doesn't pin. This means:

- `.vc-config.toml` says `account = "work"` → `vc-x1 init --account home foo` produces a `work` account repo. CLI loses.
- `.vc-config.toml` says `[global] config-path = "/etc/team.toml"` → `vc-x1 ... --global-config ~/mine.toml ...` loads `/etc/team.toml`. CLI loses there too.
- "Escape hatch" for ad-hoc deviations is `--no-local-config` (skip the whole local layer for this invocation).

I agree with this once I see it stated cleanly — it's the *project sovereignty* model, like `.editorconfig` or `direnv`. CLI tools usually go the other way, but those are user-facing convention-only; `vc-x1`'s `.vc-config.toml` is a deliberate per-project commitment that gets checked in, so honoring it over CLI is consistent.

**Circular global → global**: agreed, "tough shit." Loader detects a cycle (visited-set) and errors clearly; the recovery is "fix your config." No code complexity to prevent it.

**One open question: env-var placement.** With local > CLI established, where do env-vars sit? Three reasonable options:

- (a) **Local > env-var > CLI > global > error** — env-var is "session-default," still below local but above CLI. Matches "env-vars are a flavor of config, not a flavor of CLI."
- (b) **Local > CLI > env-var > global > error** — env-var is below CLI (closer to global). Useful when you want CLI to win over your shell's `VC_X1_*` setup.
- (c) **Local > env-var > global, env-var coequal with CLI** — env-var and CLI tied, one-of enforced.

The bot thinks (a) — env-var sits *with* config files (it's "ambient state"), not with CLI (which is "this invocation"). Local-config-says-X / env-var-says-Y → local wins because it's project-specific; env-var-says-X / CLI-says-Y → env-var wins because env-var was set deliberately and CLI is the per-invocation tweak.

But (a) means a typed `--account home` loses to `export VC_X1_ACCOUNT=work` in a shell — surprising to some users. (b) avoids that surprise. Your call.

Confirm (a)/(b)/(c) and I'll write up the resolution chain as a `### Resolution chain` subsection under `## Feature axes`, including:

- The 4 layers + error floor with chosen order
- `--no-local-config` / `--no-global-config` escape hatches
- `VC_X1_<KEY>` naming convention with the per-axis table
- "Circular global → global = user error" note
- Cross-link to A1 / A3 / A4 / A5 to show how each axis resolves through the chain

> DEVELOPER

No miss-interpretation, my opinion is: CLI > ENV > Local > Global (i.e. CLI can override everything and is GOD)

> AGENT

Got it — CLI > ENV > Local > Global > Error. Standard precedence, no exceptions; local *can express* any field (no carve-outs) but CLI still wins when both are set. My earlier confusion — I read "override every CLI" as "win over CLI" rather than "express a value for any CLI knob." Cleared up.

> AGENT

Welcome back. Picking up A2 — and I think A2 *collapses* given what we decided in A1. Let me lay out the case.

**Recap of A1 decisions** (so we're aligned):
- POR never reads or writes the workspace `.vc-config.toml`. Today's degenerate write gets dropped.
- DUAL is *detected* by the workspace `.vc-config.toml` (specifically `[workspace] other-repo`). It's the file's whole purpose.

**Consequence for A2** (`.vc-config.toml` write):
- Under A1=por: file doesn't exist. A2 is non-applicable.
- Under A1=dual: file must exist (it's how dual is detected). A2 is also non-applicable — it's always written.

So A2 isn't an independent axis at all — it's **perfectly correlated with A1**. Presence-of-`.vc-config.toml` == "this is dual."

**The `--config <path>` flag today does two things:**

1. `--config none` — skip the write. Useful only under por; under the new design por never writes it anyway, so this becomes a no-op (or rejected).
2. `--config <path>` — write content from `<path>` instead of the canned template. Useful **only if** we want non-default content in `.vc-config.toml` (e.g., a non-`.claude` companion dir name, custom workspace metadata).

The bot thinks #2 is **solving a problem we haven't stated**. Today `.claude` is hard-coded everywhere; the canned content `[workspace] path = "/", other-repo = ".claude"` is universal for dual. There's no current use case for custom-content workspace files.

**Initial decision for A2:**

- **Drop A2 as an independent axis.** It's degenerate post-A1.
- **Drop `--config <path>` entirely.** No demand for custom workspace metadata; the canned content is universal for dual. If a future need surfaces (renaming `.claude`, multi-companion workspaces), add it back as part of that feature, not as a speculative knob.
- **Keep `--config none` as a clap-rejected combination** (or just remove the flag). With por never writing the file, there's nothing for `none` to suppress.

**Open question** — am I missing a use case for custom-content workspace files? Two candidates I can think of:

- **Renaming `.claude`** — a user wanting their bot-repo to be named `.bot` or `.session` instead. Would require unhardcoding `.claude` throughout `push.rs` / `clone.rs` / etc. — a much bigger lift than just accepting a custom template.
- **Future workspace fields** — `[workspace] ochid-format` or similar, for the forks-multi-user work. Speculative; that work hasn't started.

If neither lands as a need, A2 stays collapsed. If you've got a use case I'm not seeing, surface it now.

**My recommendation: collapse A2.** Move on to A3?

> AGENT

I like the *direction* — generalizing template seeding to one mechanism is cleaner than inventing `--config <path>` + `--gitignore <path>` + future per-file flags. But I want to push back on scope before we fold this into A2.

**The proposal's real shape:** you're replacing all template-related knobs (`--config`, `--gitignore`, and `--use-template`) with one composable templating system. That's its own design — bigger than A2's collapse.

**What `--init-from` / `--init-from-recursive` subsumes:**

- `--config <path>` → `--init-from=<src>/.vc-config.toml`
- `--gitignore <path>` → `--init-from=<src>/.gitignore`
- `--use-template code-tpl/` → `--init-from-recursive=code-tpl/*`
- Any future "seed file X" need → just point `--init-from` at it

That's a real win. One mechanism, no per-file flag explosion.

**Open questions I'd surface before designing:**

1. **Glob vs regex syntax.** You wrote "non-recursive but regex would be supported" — what's the exact syntax? Shell-glob (`*.toml`, expanded by the shell, vc-x1 sees the expanded list)? Or regex inside the path string (`<dir>/.*\.toml`, parsed by vc-x1)? I lean shell-glob — let the shell do globbing, vc-x1 just gets file paths. Regex-in-path is unusual and inconsistent with `cp` / `rsync` conventions users expect.

2. **Per-side targeting under dual.** `vc-x1 init foo` produces *two* destinations (code repo + `.claude/`). Where does `--init-from=<src>` land?
   - (a) Code by default; `--init-from-bot=<src>` for the companion.
   - (b) `--init-from=<src>::<dest>` syntax where `<dest>` is `code` / `bot`.
   - (c) Source layout mirrors target (`<src>/code/...` → code repo, `<src>/.claude/...` → companion).
   I lean (c) — the source dir's own layout encodes the target. No flag explosion, naturally extensible.

3. **Conflict resolution.** Multiple `--init-from` invocations land the same file (`a/.gitignore` and `b/.gitignore`). Last wins? First wins? Error?

4. **Canned writes vs templates.** Today init writes canned `.vc-config.toml` + `.gitignore`. With templating:
   - (a) Templates land first, canned fills only what templates didn't provide. (Templates "override" canned.)
   - (b) Any `--init-from` suppresses canned entirely.
   - (c) Canned never runs if templates exist.
   I lean (a) — predictable, additive, no special-casing.

5. **Mandatory dual fields.** Dual *requires* `.vc-config.toml` with `[workspace] other-repo`. If user's template lacks it, init should detect and fail with a clear error (not write a broken dual).

**Scope recommendation:**

Don't fold the templating design into A2. The audit doc should stay focused on **topology parity** — the question that motivated the cycle. Templating is its own feature.

Concrete proposal:

- **A2 collapse stays as-is**: write of `.vc-config.toml` is perfectly correlated with A1. Drop A2 as an independent axis.
- **Drop the speculative `--config <path>` capability** from A2's section in the doc.
- **Add a stub** to the audit doc (a brief paragraph under A2 or in the Gap list) noting: "the *capability* `--config <path>` was meant to serve (custom workspace metadata) is deferred to a broader init-templating design — see `notes/init-templating.md` (TBD)."
- **Spin `notes/init-templating.md`** as a separate design note capturing the `--init-from` / `--init-from-recursive` proposal, the questions above, and the eventual rollout. This becomes its own cycle in 0.62.0+ — independent of the parity equalization work.

Either I create the stub `notes/init-templating.md` now (within `-4` or a new commit) with the proposal + open questions, or we defer the note entirely to that future cycle. **My preference: create the stub now while context is fresh** — drops it into the design space so future-you finds it. ~30 lines.

Thoughts? Move on to A3 either way?

> AGENT

Got it. Let me confirm interpretations + raise one nuance before writing the stub:

1. **Glob** ✓ (shell-glob, vc-x1 receives expanded paths).
2. **Scope** — I'll go with the variant-in-command-name approach: `--init-from-code=<glob>` and `--init-from-bot=<glob>`, with bare `--init-from=<glob>` defaulting to code (since por only has code). Rules out the `--scope` clash with the existing runtime `--scope` flag. Clean and clap-friendly.
3. **Last wins + warning on collision** ✓.
4. **Any `--init-from*` suppresses canned writes entirely** ✓ — clean. User opts into full control once they engage templating.
5. **One pushback**: you said *warning* if `.vc-config.toml` lookup fails after copy. For **dual specifically** I think it should be a **hard error**, not a warning:

   - Dual without `.vc-config.toml` is a broken workspace — `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` won't detect the topology, every downstream subcommand will treat it as por.
   - Warn-and-create gets you a workspace that *looks* dual (has `.claude/`) but *acts* por at runtime. Surprising and hard to debug.
   - Refusing to create a broken workspace is the right call.

   For **por**: no `.vc-config.toml` is expected; no warning needed.

   Concretely: after `--init-from*` runs, if A1=dual and the resolved workspace config file (default name `.vc-config.toml`, overridable via [some future mechanism]) doesn't exist or doesn't have `[workspace] other-repo`, error out before continuing. **Warn** vs **error**: your call.

**Default-name overridability** (your #5 hedge): I think the workspace-config filename should stay fixed at `.vc-config.toml` for now — overriding it ripples through every subcommand's lookup code. If someone wants a non-default name later, that's a real feature with real cost; don't bake the flexibility in speculatively. So the dual check becomes "after copy, does `.vc-config.toml` exist in the code dir with `[workspace] other-repo` set" — fixed name, no override mechanism.

**Plan:**

1. Create `notes/init-templating.md` — design stub capturing what we've negotiated. ~50 lines.
2. Update A2's section in the audit doc — note A2 collapses and link to the templating stub.
3. Move to A3.

That's a `-4` Work commit. Sound good? (If yes, I'll also need a quick call on the dual warn-vs-error.)

> DEVELOPER

That's fine, but slight push-back on your push-back, it maybe the user plans to provide it post initialization, I suggest warning but on a future invocation of `vc-x1` in --dual mode it would be a hard-error.

> AGENT

Accepted — that's the better shape. "Init is for setup; downstream commands enforce correctness" is consistent with how the rest of the chain works (e.g., `default_scope` errors when `--scope=bot` requested but no `other-repo`).

Implementation note worth capturing: `default_scope` today *silently* returns `Scope([Code])` when `.vc-config.toml` is missing. That's exactly the "looks dual, acts por" failure mode. To make your warn-then-error work, `default_scope` (or a new check) needs to detect *broken-dual* state — `.claude/` exists in the workspace but `.vc-config.toml` doesn't — and refuse. Today's silent fallback masks the very thing we'd want to catch.

Writing the stub + updating A2 now.

Generalizes seed-file handling\nin `vc-x1 init` (and by extension `vc-x1 clone`) into one\ncomposable templating mechanism that subsumes today's\n`--config <path>` (deprecated under A2 collapse — see\n[[1]]) and a hypothetical `--gitignore <path>`.\n\n## Goal\n\nOne mechanism for \"seed files into the workspace at init\ntime\" rather than per-file flags. The user supplies one or\nmore source paths; init copies them into the new workspace\nduring the seed phase, before commits. Subsumes today's\n`--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` as the natural shorthand.\n\n## Surface\n\n- `--init-from-code=<glob>` — copy files matched by\n  `<glob>` into the code repo (non-recursive).\n- `--init-from-bot=<glob>` — same, into the `.claude/`\n  companion. Only meaningful under dual.\n- `--init-from=<glob>` — shorthand for `--init-from-code`\n  (since por only has code, and dual users typically seed\n  code more often than bot).\n- `--init-from-recursive=<glob>` /\n  `--init-from-code-recursive=<glob>` /\n  `--init-from-bot-recursive=<glob>` — recursive variants.\n\nEach flag accepts a **shell glob** (expansion done by the\nshell; `vc-x1` receives the expanded path list). Each may\nbe specified multiple times — the result is the additive\nunion of all sources. Order matters for collisions (see\nbelow).\n\n## Behavior\n\n- **Copy semantics** — files only; preserves relative\n  layout under the source root.\n- **Collision: last writer wins, with a warning.** If two\n  `--init-from*` flags resolve to the same destination\n  file, the later one overwrites the earlier; `vc-x1`\n  emits a warning naming both sources. The bot thinks\n  warnings are right here — silent overwrite is a\n  footgun; erroring would block valid layering use cases\n  (a base template + per-project tweaks).\n- **Canned writes suppressed when templating is engaged.**\n  Today `init` writes a canned `.vc-config.toml` and\n  `.gitignore`. With any `--init-from*` present, **both\n  canned writes are suppressed entirely**. The user opted\n  in to full control of the seeded content; init doesn't\n  layer canned defaults underneath their template.\n- **Dual post-templating check (deferred).** After\n  templating completes for a dual workspace, if\n  `.vc-config.toml` is missing or lacks\n  `[workspace] other-repo`, init **warns** (does not\n  error). The workspace looks dual (has `.claude/`) but\n  acts por at runtime; downstream subcommands enforce\n  the invariant. See [Deferred-validation](#deferred-validation).\n\n## Deferred-validation\n\nInit's `.vc-config.toml` check is a **warning**, not an\nerror, to support workflows where the user plans to\nsupply the config post-init (e.g. checking in the\nworkspace shell first, then populating config files in\na follow-up commit).\n\nThe error happens at the **first downstream invocation**:\n`default_scope` (or a new explicit check) detects the\nbroken-dual state — `.claude/` exists but `.vc-config.toml`\ndoesn't (or lacks `other-repo`) — and refuses to proceed\nwith a clear error pointing at the missing file.\n\nToday `default_scope` silently falls back to\n`Scope([Code])` when `.vc-config.toml` is absent\n(`src/common.rs:594`). That silent fallback is exactly\nthe \"looks dual, acts por\" surprise this design avoids;\nfixing it is a prerequisite for the post-init templating\nflow.\n\n## Fixed filename\n\n`.vc-config.toml` stays the fixed name; overriding it is\ndeferred until a real use case surfaces (would ripple\nthrough every subcommand's lookup code — `default_scope`,\n`find_workspace_root_from`, `validate-desc`, `fix-desc`,\n`push`, `clone`, …).\n\n## Subsumes\n\n- `--config <path>` — never built; A2's collapse left\n  the capability un-claimed. Equivalent under templating:\n  `--init-from=<src>/.vc-config.toml`.\n- `--gitignore <path>` — never built; equivalent under\n  templating: `--init-from=<src>/.gitignore`.\n- `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` — today's coarse\n  whole-repo template flag. Subsumed by recursive\n  variants:\n  - `--use-template code-tpl/` →\n    `--init-from-code-recursive=code-tpl/*`.\n  - `--use-template code-tpl/,bot-tpl/` → two flags:\n    `--init-from-code-recursive=code-tpl/*` +\n    `--init-from-bot-recursive=bot-tpl/*`.\n\nThe bot thinks `--use-template` can be retired once\ntemplating ships, but a back-compat shim that translates\nold form → new form is cheap and worth keeping for one\nrelease cycle.\n\n## Open questions (resolve at design time)\n\n- **Variable substitution.** Should the copy step support\n  variable substitution (`{{name}}`, `{{account}}`)? The\n  bot thinks no — keep the mechanism a pure copy. Users\n  who need substitution can pre-process and pass the\n  result. Adding substitution invites a templating-language\n  rabbit hole.\n- **Symbolic links and executable bits.** Preserve, or\n  flatten? Probably preserve — `cp -a` semantics. Worth\n  confirming.\n- **Source-not-found behavior.** Hard error (typo\n  protection) or soft skip (let globs return empty)?\n  Probably hard error — a missing source is almost\n  always user mistake. Empty glob expansion (the shell\n  passing zero paths) is fine.\n- **Interaction with `vc-x1 clone`.** Clone seeds from an\n  existing remote, so templating overlays *on top of* the\n  cloned content. Same flag set, same collision rules.\n\n## See also\n\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A2`\n  — the collapse that motivates this design [[1]].\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A5`\n  — `--use-template` today; this design subsumes it.\n\n# References\n\n[1]: /notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md#a2-vc-configtoml-write\n"

Summary:

- **`notes/init-templating.md`** (new, 142 lines) — design stub for the templating system. Goal, surface (`--init-from`, `--init-from-code`, `--init-from-bot`, `*-recursive` variants), behavior (last-wins + warning, canned-writes suppressed when engaged, deferred dual validation), fixed `.vc-config.toml` name, subsumes `--config`/`--gitignore`/`--use-template`, open questions (variable substitution, symlinks, source-not-found, clone interaction).
- **`notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md`** — A2 section rewritten to reflect collapse; cross-link to `init-templating.md` via new `[[3]]` ref.

> DEVELOPER

We need to define what a "template" is, I'm not sure although I'm guessing template simply means unspecified things take on some "default" value defined in "local/global configs" ?

> AGENT

Good catch — "template" is overloaded and I was sloppy. There are actually two distinct concepts in play, and they're easy to conflate:

**Concept 1: Template = files seeded into the workspace.**

Today's `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` and our proposed `--init-from*` are both **pure file-copy operations**. They take source paths, copy the contents into the new workspace, done. No variable substitution, no logic. The "template" is just a directory of pre-canned files (`.gitignore`, `README.md`, `LICENSE`, language-specific scaffolding, etc.).

Your `init-templating.md` open question explicitly says: *"Should the copy step support variable substitution? The bot thinks no — keep the mechanism a pure copy."*

So under this design, "template" is barely justified as a term — it's really just **seeding** (or **scaffolding**). The word "template" implies parameterization, which our design doesn't do.

**Concept 2: Config defaults = unspecified flag values resolve from configs.**

This is what your "guess" describes — and it's a **completely separate mechanism**, already covered by the `### Resolution chain` we wrote (CLI > ENV > Local > Global > Error). When the user runs `vc-x1 init foo` without `--account`, the resolver walks the chain looking for `[default].account` in the configs. That's value resolution, not file seeding.

These two concepts interact but don't overlap:

| | Concept 1 (Seeding) | Concept 2 (Defaults) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Object | Files | Flag values |
| Mechanism | `cp -r` from source path | Lookup in resolution chain |
| Surface | `--init-from*` | `--account`, `--private`, etc. |
| Result | New files in workspace | Resolved CLI flag value |

**My recommendation:** rename the design to avoid the conflation.

- File: `notes/init-templating.md` → `notes/init-seeding.md` (or `init-scaffolding.md`)
- Concept: "init templating" → "init seeding"
- Flags: keep `--init-from*` (already neutral about "template")
- Update A2/A5 sections + ref `[3]` in the audit doc to point at the new name.

If you confirm, I'll rename in `-4` before commit. (Or, if you prefer to keep "template" because there's a future where the mechanism DOES do substitution, we can keep it — but the design as-written is purely seeding.)

**Future templating-with-substitution** is a separate, more powerful mechanism (think `cookiecutter`, `mustache`). Not in current scope. If we ever build it, it'd layer on top of seeding — a "templated source" gets substitution applied before copy.

**Want the rename?** Then move to A3.

> DEVELOPER

discussion; - Concept: "init copying" or just "copying"?

> AGENT

Discussion thoughts:

**"copying"** alone (no prefix) — captures the mechanism literally but loses the *when*. The reader sees "copying" and asks "copying what, when?" The doc/concept needs context.

**"init copying"** — accurate, just-the-facts. Pro: no semantic baggage, no metaphor to chase. Con: flat — "init copying" tells you what *happens* but not what *purpose* it serves. Reads slightly clunky as a noun phrase (compare "init seeding" which scans cleaner).

**"init seeding"** — my preference. "Seed" is the established term for "initial files filling out a new thing" (database seeds, project seeds). Reads naturally; implies purpose; doesn't overpromise (no parameterization implied).

**"init scaffolding"** — also viable, but "scaffolding" tends to imply *structure generation* (Rails generators, Yeoman) — which is closer to substitution-aware templating than to pure file copy. Slight mismatch with our pure-copy design.

**One related consideration:** is this mechanism *intrinsically* init-bound, or could it extend? E.g., a future `vc-x1 sync --copy-from=<glob>` to layer files into an existing workspace. If extension is plausible, bind the concept name to *what it does* (copy / seed) and leave init-vs-not as a separate axis:

- Concept = "file seeding" (or "file copying")
- Today's surface = `vc-x1 init --init-from*`
- Future surface = `vc-x1 <whatever> --copy-from*`

The bot thinks **"seeding"** at the concept level + **`--init-from*`** at the flag level reads best. Doc would be `notes/seeding.md` (or `notes/file-seeding.md` if "seeding" alone is too sparse).

**One more option worth surfacing**: skip the metaphor entirely. Call the doc `notes/init-from.md` — names the *flag family*, not a concept. Pro: zero metaphor, zero ambiguity. Con: prose about it has to keep saying "the `--init-from` mechanism" which is verbose.

**Ranking, my preference:**
1. `notes/seeding.md` (concept-level, extensible)
2. `notes/init-from.md` (flag-level, zero metaphor)
3. `notes/init-seeding.md` (init-bound but readable)
4. `notes/init-copying.md` (works, but flat)

Your call. (Or push back on any of these.)

> DEVELOPER

"seeding" to me implies there is growth a seed grows. This is copying, hence I like copying.

> AGENT

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/copying.md",
  "content": "# copying (design stub)\n\nForward-looking design capture from the 0.61.0 parity audit\ncycle. No implementation yet — concrete work lands when a\nfollow-on cycle picks it up.\n\nGeneralizes \"copy these files into the new workspace at\ninit time\" into one composable mechanism that subsumes\ntoday's `--config <path>` (deprecated under A2 collapse —\nsee [[1]]), a hypothetical `--gitignore <path>`, and\ntoday's `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>`.\n\nThe mechanism is **pure file copy** — no variable\nsubstitution, no transformation. The bot deliberately\navoided \"templating\" / \"seeding\" as concept names; \"seed\"\nimplies growth and \"template\" implies parameterization,\nneither of which this mechanism does. It just copies.\n\n## Goal\n\nOne mechanism for \"copy these files into the workspace at\ninit time\" rather than per-file flags. The user supplies\none or more source paths; init copies them into the new\nworkspace during the copy phase, before commits.\n\n## Surface\n\n- `--init-from-code=<glob>` — copy files matched by\n  `<glob>` into the code repo (non-recursive).\n- `--init-from-bot=<glob>` — same, into the `.claude/`\n  companion. Only meaningful under dual.\n- `--init-from=<glob>` — shorthand for `--init-from-code`\n  (since por only has code, and dual users typically copy\n  into code more often than bot).\n- `--init-from-recursive=<glob>` /\n  `--init-from-code-recursive=<glob>` /\n  `--init-from-bot-recursive=<glob>` — recursive variants.\n\nEach flag accepts a **shell glob** (expansion done by the\nshell; `vc-x1` receives the expanded path list). Each may\nbe specified multiple times — the result is the additive\nunion of all sources. Order matters for collisions (see\nbelow).\n\n## Behavior\n\n- **Copy semantics** — files only; preserves relative\n  layout under the source root. The bot's working\n  assumption is `cp -a` semantics (preserve symbolic\n  links and executable bits); confirm at design time.\n- **Collision: last writer wins, with a warning.** If two\n  `--init-from*` flags resolve to the same destination\n  file, the later one overwrites the earlier; `vc-x1`\n  emits a warning naming both sources. The bot thinks\n  warnings are right here — silent overwrite is a\n  footgun; erroring would block valid layering use cases\n  (a base directory + per-project tweaks).\n- **Canned writes suppressed when copying is engaged.**\n  Today `init` writes a canned `.vc-config.toml` and\n  `.gitignore`. With any `--init-from*` present, **both\n  canned writes are suppressed entirely**. The user opted\n  in to full control of the copied content; init doesn't\n  layer canned defaults underneath.\n- **Dual post-copy check (deferred).** After copying\n  completes for a dual workspace, if `.vc-config.toml`\n  is missing or lacks `[workspace] other-repo`, init\n  **warns** (does not error). The workspace looks dual\n  (has `.claude/`) but acts por at runtime; downstream\n  subcommands enforce the invariant. See\n  [Deferred-validation](#deferred-validation).\n\n## Deferred-validation\n\nInit's `.vc-config.toml` check is a **warning**, not an\nerror, to support workflows where the user plans to\nsupply the config post-init (e.g. checking in the\nworkspace shell first, then populating config files in a\nfollow-up commit).\n\nThe error happens at the **first downstream invocation**:\n`default_scope` (or a new explicit check) detects the\nbroken-dual state — `.claude/` exists but `.vc-config.toml`\ndoesn't (or lacks `other-repo`) — and refuses to proceed\nwith a clear error pointing at the missing file.\n\nToday `default_scope` silently falls back to\n`Scope([Code])` when `.vc-config.toml` is absent\n(`src/common.rs:594`). That silent fallback is exactly\nthe \"looks dual, acts por\" surprise this design avoids;\nfixing it is a prerequisite for the post-init copy\nworkflow.\n\n## Fixed filename\n\n`.vc-config.toml` stays the fixed name; overriding it is\ndeferred until a real use case surfaces (would ripple\nthrough every subcommand's lookup code — `default_scope`,\n`find_workspace_root_from`, `validate-desc`, `fix-desc`,\n`push`, `clone`, …).\n\n## Subsumes\n\n- `--config <path>` — never really built; A2's collapse\n  left the capability un-claimed. Equivalent under\n  copying: `--init-from=<src>/.vc-config.toml`.\n- `--gitignore <path>` — never built; equivalent under\n  copying: `--init-from=<src>/.gitignore`.\n- `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` — today's coarse\n  whole-repo flag. Subsumed by recursive variants:\n  - `--use-template code-tpl/` →\n    `--init-from-code-recursive=code-tpl/*`.\n  - `--use-template code-tpl/,bot-tpl/` → two flags:\n    `--init-from-code-recursive=code-tpl/*` +\n    `--init-from-bot-recursive=bot-tpl/*`.\n\nThe bot thinks `--use-template` can be retired once\ncopying ships, but a back-compat shim that translates\nold form → new form is cheap and worth keeping for one\nrelease cycle.\n\n## Naming rationale\n\nThe mechanism is named **copying** (not \"templating,\" not\n\"seeding,\" not \"scaffolding\") because all three of those\nimply something the mechanism doesn't do:\n\n- \"Templating\" implies parameterization / variable\n  substitution. We deliberately don't do that.\n- \"Seeding\" implies growth (a seed grows into a plant);\n  the copied files don't grow — they sit where they\n  landed.\n- \"Scaffolding\" implies project-structure generation\n  (Rails generators, Yeoman); the mechanism is just\n  `cp -r`, no structure-aware logic.\n\n\"Copying\" is literal: source files go to destination\npaths. The flag family `--init-from*` keeps the verb out\nof the surface — the flag describes *what* (source-to-\ndestination), not *what kind of operation*. If a future\nmechanism does layer substitution on top of copy, it's a\nseparate feature with its own name.\n\n## Open questions (resolve at design time)\n\n- **Variable substitution.** Should the copy step support\n  `{{name}}` / `{{account}}` substitution? The bot\n  thinks **no** — keep the mechanism pure copy. Users\n  who need substitution can pre-process and pass the\n  result. Adding substitution invites a substitution-\n  language rabbit hole and breaks the \"copying\" framing\n  we just settled on.\n- **Symbolic links and executable bits.** Preserve, or\n  flatten? Probably preserve — `cp -a` semantics.\n  Worth confirming at design time.\n- **Source-not-found behavior.** Hard error (typo\n  protection) or soft skip (let globs return empty)?\n  Probably hard error — a missing source is almost\n  always user mistake. Empty glob expansion (the shell\n  passing zero paths) is fine.\n- **Interaction with `vc-x1 clone`.** Clone seeds from\n  an existing remote, so copying overlays *on top of*\n  the cloned content. Same flag set, same collision\n  rules.\n- **Mechanism beyond init?** A future\n  `vc-x1 <subcommand> --copy-from=<glob>` to layer\n  files into an existing workspace would be the same\n  mechanism with a different host command. The bot\n  thinks defer until a real use surfaces; `--init-from*`\n  is the only surface today.\n\n## See also\n\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A2`\n  — the collapse that motivates this design [[1]].\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A5`\n  — `--use-template` today; this design subsumes it.\n\n# References\n\n[1]: /notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md#a2-vc-configtoml-write--collapsed\n"
}
```

Equivalent under\n  copying: `--init-from=<src>/.vc-config.toml`.\n- `--gitignore <path>` — never built; equivalent under\n  copying: `--init-from=<src>/.gitignore`.\n- `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` — today's coarse\n  whole-repo flag. We deliberately don't do that.\n- \"Seeding\" implies growth (a seed grows into a plant);\n  the copied files don't grow — they sit where they\n  landed.\n- \"Scaffolding\" implies project-structure generation\n  (Rails generators, Yeoman); the mechanism is just\n  `cp -r`, no structure-aware logic.\n\n\"Copying\" is literal: source files go to destination\npaths. The flag family `--init-from*` keeps the verb out\nof the surface — the flag describes *what* (source-to-\ndestination), not *what kind of operation*. If a future\nmechanism does layer substitution on top of copy, it's a\nseparate feature with its own name.\n\n## Open questions (resolve at design time)\n\n- **Variable substitution.** Should the copy step support\n  `{{name}}` / `{{account}}` substitution? The bot\n  thinks **no** — keep the mechanism pure copy. Users\n  who need substitution can pre-process and pass the\n  result. Adding substitution invites a substitution-\n  language rabbit hole and breaks the \"copying\" framing\n  we just settled on.\n- **Symbolic links and executable bits.** Preserve, or\n  flatten? Probably preserve — `cp -a` semantics.\n  Worth confirming at design time.\n- **Source-not-found behavior.** Hard error (typo\n  protection) or soft skip (let globs return empty)?\n  Probably hard error — a missing source is almost\n  always user mistake. Empty glob expansion (the shell\n  passing zero paths) is fine.\n- **Interaction with `vc-x1 clone`.** Clone seeds from\n  an existing remote, so copying overlays *on top of*\n  the cloned content. Same flag set, same collision\n  rules.\n- **Mechanism beyond init?** A future\n  `vc-x1 <subcommand> --copy-from=<glob>` to layer\n  files into an existing workspace would be the same\n  mechanism with a different host command. The bot\n  thinks defer until a real use surfaces; `--init-from*`\n  is the only surface today.\n\n## See also\n\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A2`\n  — the collapse that motivates this design [[1]].\n- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md > ## Feature axes > A5`\n  — `--use-template` today; this design subsumes it.\n\n# References\n\n[1]: /notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md#a2-vc-configtoml-write--collapsed\n"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

maybe "copying.md" should be "init-from.md"?

> AGENT

Honest opinion: I lean **keep `copying.md`**, but the case for `init-from.md` is real. Let me lay it out and you decide.

**Case for `init-from.md`:**

- A user reading `vc-x1 init --help` sees `--init-from-code`, wonders what it does, greps `init-from`. A file with that name is the obvious target.
- Names the **surface** (flags) the user actually types.
- Existing precedent: `cycle-protocol.md` is named after the workflow shape (which is more concept than surface, granted), but `jj-revsets.md` is named after the surface primitive. Mixed precedent.

**Case for `copying.md`:**

- The doc spends most of its content on **concept-level design** — semantics, collision rules, canned-write suppression, deferred validation, naming rationale. The flag surface is one section. Naming the file after one section doesn't reflect the file's center of gravity.
- We just settled on "copying" as the concept name. Internal consistency: concept name → file name.
- If the flag family ever extends (e.g., a future `vc-x1 sync --copy-from`), `init-from.md` becomes misleading. `copying.md` stays accurate.
- Discoverability is symmetric — the file contains both "copying" (concept) and `--init-from*` (flag), so grep finds it either way.

**The tipping factor for me:** the doc has a whole `## Naming rationale` section explaining why we chose "copying" over "templating" / "seeding" / "scaffolding." That section reads weird if the file is named `init-from.md` — it's defending a concept name in a file named for the flag.

That said, if your instinct is `init-from.md`, it's a defensible call — and the user-grep argument is the strongest counter to mine.

**Want to keep `copying.md`, or rename to `init-from.md`?** Either is fine; the work to flip is small.

> DEVELOPER

Actually we should put these new file in a directory maybe, "design-cli/" or "architecture-cli/" or "params/" ...

> AGENT

Worth thinking about — design notes are accumulating. Let me lay out what I see and a recommendation.

**What's in `notes/` today** (skimming):

- **Forward-looking design** — `por-dual-parity.md`, `forks-multi-user.md`, now `copying.md`, the audit doc (border case — analysis + design).
- **Workflow / process** — `cycle-protocol.md`, `substep-test.sh`.
- **Reference** — `jj-revsets.md`.
- **State** — `todo.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, `bugs.md`, `done.md`, `README.md`.
- **Historical** — `chores/` (already subdir-ed).

A directory only helps if the rule is **clean**. The grouping I find cleanest:

**`notes/design/`** — pre-implementation forward-looking design docs.

- `copying.md` (this cycle)
- `por-dual-parity.md` (existing)
- `forks-multi-user.md` (existing)
- *Maybe* `por-dual-parity-audit.md` (border case — it's a snapshot + analysis + decisions, not pure forward design)

**Stays in `notes/`:**

- Workflow / process (`cycle-protocol.md`, `substep-test.sh`)
- Reference (`jj-revsets.md`)
- State files (`todo.md`, `bugs.md`, `done.md`, etc.)
- `chores/` (already its own subdir)

**Pushback on the alternatives you offered:**

- **`design-cli/`** — too narrow. The CLI is one surface; design also covers data shapes, protocols, semantics. `forks-multi-user.md` is about ochid trailer structure, not CLI; would feel wrong in a CLI-only bucket.
- **`architecture-cli/`** — "architecture" implies *system structure* (which lives in `/ARCHITECTURE.md` at repo root). These are feature designs, not architecture.
- **`params/`** — way too narrow. The docs cover semantics, validation, workflow — not just flag parameters.

**`notes/design/`** generalizes cleanly and matches the existing one-word subdir pattern (`chores/`).

**Two scope concerns to flag before we do it:**

1. **Mid-cycle reorg adds churn.** Every link to `por-dual-parity.md` updates (currently referenced in `todo.md`, `notes/README.md`, the audit doc, and `forks-multi-user.md` likely). Tractable, but real.
2. **Should this be its own cycle?** 0.61.0 has been adding scope all along, so doing it here is consistent — but a docs-reorg cycle is a clean atomic thing. Arguments either way.

**My recommendation:** do the reorg, do it now (the scope is small and the new design notes are easier to land in the right home from the start than to move later), name it `notes/design/`. Three files move: `copying.md`, `por-dual-parity.md`, `forks-multi-user.md`. The audit doc stays in `notes/` (it's a 0.61.0 cycle output, not a forward-design stub; it'll get a chores section link and that's its primary anchor).

This becomes `-5` in the ladder, separate from `-4`'s A2 collapse work. Commit `-4` first (the work we just did with `copying.md` at the root), then `-5` is the reorg move (`copying.md` + the two existing files into `notes/design/`), with link updates.

**Sound right?** Or rename `copying.md` → `init-from.md` first, then `-5` does the reorg? Or skip the reorg entirely for this cycle?

ATM, we're talking about "cli" not implementation so "design-cli/" or "design/cli"

> AGENT

Fair pushback. Reframing the scope as "CLI design specifically" tightens what moves and clarifies the rule. Let me recalibrate.

**Flat (`design-cli/`) vs nested (`design/cli/`):**

I lean **flat `notes/design-cli/`**:

- One level deep, consistent with the existing `notes/chores/` pattern.
- Doesn't presuppose siblings (`design-data/`, `design-protocol/`, …). YAGNI — if `design-data/` ever materializes, decide nesting *then*.
- The user reading the tree sees `notes/design-cli/` and knows exactly what's there with no extra click.

**Nested has one real benefit** — if multiple `design-*` siblings emerge, `design/cli/` + `design/data/` is structurally cleaner than two top-level dirs. But that's speculative; nothing forces siblings to materialize.

**With the tighter "CLI design" scope, what moves changes:**

- `notes/copying.md` → `notes/design-cli/copying.md` ✓ (about `--init-from*` flags)
- `notes/por-dual-parity.md` → `notes/design-cli/por-dual-parity.md` ✓ (about `--por` / `--dual` flags)
- `notes/forks-multi-user.md` — **stays in `notes/`**. It's about ochid trailer *data structure*, not CLI surface. Your original critique-of-my-critique was right.
- `notes/por-dual-parity-audit.md` — **stays in `notes/`**. It's a 0.61.0 cycle output (audit + analysis), not a forward CLI design stub. Has substantial non-CLI content (commonality analysis of internal code structure).

Just **two files move**. The audit doc's links to `copying.md` and `por-dual-parity.md` get updated. Cleaner reorg than I sketched before.

**Proposed shape for `-5`:**

1. `mkdir notes/design-cli/`

7. Update `notes/README.md` if it references either file (likely does).

`-5` committed as `c9ad35ef`. Now A3.

---

**A3: Remote provisioning** — recap of where the audit doc stands today:

- **States**: `github-create` (default; gh CLI creates `<account>/<name>`) | `github-skip` (URL is non-GitHub, pre-existing) | `local-bare` (`--repo local=<dir>` makes `git init --bare`) | `none` (doesn't exist today).
- **Today's surface**: `--repo <cat>[=<val>]` (`remote` / `local` categories), `--private` (GitHub visibility), `--push-retries` / `--push-retry-delay`.
- **Gap**: no way to spell "no remote" — a user wanting just a local working repo with no upstream has no clean expression.

**Recap of decisions that already shape A3** (from A1 + Resolution chain):

- Resolution chain CLI > ENV > Local > Global > Error applies to **every axis**, including `--repo`, `--account`, `--private`.
- Error floor: if no layer resolves a required field, error with the exact key to set.
- POR ignores workspace `.vc-config.toml` entirely; user-config still consulted.

---

**Initial decisions to make for A3:**

**(D1) Add a `none` state to the remote axis.**

Today `--repo` resolves to `remote` or `local`; no `none`. Proposal: add a third category so `--repo none` means "do not provision any remote." The local working repo has no `origin` set.

Use case: developer wants a workspace they'll publish later (or never). Today they have to `--repo local=<somewhere>` and discard the bare repo, or fake a URL.

Bot's recommendation: **add `none` as a third category**. Spelled `--repo none` (no value). User-config equivalent: `[account.<a>].repo.default = "none"` or top-level `[repo].default = "none"`. The bot thinks this is the cleanest extension — three peer categories (`remote` / `local` / `none`), no special-casing.

**(D2) Should `none` participate in the same account/category resolution as `remote` / `local`?**

I.e., should the user be able to say in their config `[account.scratch].repo.default = "none"` so `--account scratch` defaults to no-remote? Or is `none` a per-invocation choice only, not a defaulting concept?

Bot's recommendation: **yes, treat `none` as a first-class category**. Same resolution chain. Different accounts may want different defaults (your `home` account creates GitHub; your `scratch` account creates no remote).

**(D3) `--private` semantics when category != `github`.**

Today `--private` is GitHub-specific (private vs public visibility). Under `local` it's meaningless; under `none` it's nonsensical. Should the CLI:

- (a) Silently ignore `--private` when category isn't GitHub-ish.
- (b) Error: "`--private` only meaningful with `--repo remote=git@github.com:...`."
- (c) Reframe `--private` as a generic "private if the provider supports it" knob — GitHub → private repo, GitLab → private project, local/none → ignored.

Bot's recommendation: **(b)** — error, with a clear message. Silent-ignore is the kind of footgun this cycle has been hunting (cf. the audit's recurring "silently treats X as Y" findings).

**(D4) Should remote-create-fail recovery exist?**

Today if `gh repo create` fails (rate limit, name collision, auth), init aborts and the workspace state is partial. Should there be a `--remote-provisioned` flag that tells init "the remote already exists, skip the create step but still wire up the push"?

The audit found `RemoteCreate::Skip` exists internally for non-GitHub URLs — could be exposed as a flag.

Bot's recommendation: **defer to a follow-up cycle**, not part of A3 redesign. Today's behavior (abort on failure) is acceptable; a `--remote-provisioned` flag is a separate feature.

---

**Your turn.** Decisions on D1–D4? (Or push back on framing.)

> DEVELOPER

D1 yes, D2 yes, D3 b, D4 defer

> AGENT

All four accepted. Two more A3 nuances worth deciding before we capture and move on:

**(D5) When `--repo none`, is `--account` still required?**

Account today picks which `[account.<n>]` block to consult for `repo.category.<cat>` lookups. If `--repo none`, no category lookup needed.

- (a) Account still required (uniformity — every invocation resolves an account).
- (b) Account skipped when `--repo none` resolves (only required when there's a remote to provision).

Bot's recommendation: **(b)** — account is *meaningless* under `--repo none`. The error floor only applies to keys *needed* for the requested operation. Demanding an account for an account-less operation is friction without value.

This has a small wrinkle: the error message gets contextual. Today's "no account specified; set `[default].account`…" only fires when `--repo` resolves to a remote category. Under `--repo none` it's silent. The user has to understand "the error only fires if you need the field." The bot thinks that's the right semantics; the help-text / `--help` for `--repo` should make the dependency clear.

**(D6) Under dual, does `--repo` apply uniformly to code + bot, or per-side?**

Today: single `--repo` value drives both code and bot remote creation (same GitHub account, names derived from the workspace name).

- (a) Uniform — `--repo` applies to both sides equally (today's behavior).
- (b) Per-side — `--repo-code` / `--repo-bot` accept different categories/values.

Bot's recommendation: **(a) uniform, defer per-side**. Per-side is a real but speculative feature (e.g., "code on GitHub public, bot on GitHub private under a different account"). Today's uniform behavior is sufficient for the workflows we have. Adding `--repo-code` / `--repo-bot` later is cheap and additive — no architectural barrier.

If per-side becomes desired, the natural shape is: `--repo` as the default, `--repo-code` / `--repo-bot` as overrides per side. Same resolution chain applies independently per side.

---

**Your call on D5 and D6.** After that, A3 is fully negotiated and I'll capture the decisions into the audit doc (a new `**Decisions (0.61.0):**` subsection under A3), then we move to A4.

---

**Privacy axis** (was A4) — today's state:

- Default: **public** (silently, when category is GitHub-ish).
- Surface: `--private` boolean flag.
- Per D3 from Remote: errors when category != github-ish.

Under the explicit-required floor we locked in for Topology, the silent "public" default is inconsistent. Three positions to choose from:

**(Priv-default-1) Strict explicit-required.** No default. User must spell `--public` / `--private` on CLI, set `VC_X1_PRIVATE`, or pin `[default].private` in config. Otherwise error.

- Pro: consistent with Topology. Privacy is consequential; force the choice.
- Con: highest friction. New user runs `vc-x1 init foo`, gets an error before they understand what's being asked.

**(Priv-default-2) Default-private.** If not specified, create private. User spells `--public` to opt out.

- Pro: security-safe default. Accidental public-publish of secrets / credentials is a real failure mode; private-by-default protects.
- Pro: lower friction than (1). Naked `init` works; user only types a flag when they want public.
- Con: surprising change from today's behavior for existing users.

**(Priv-default-3) Default-public.** Today's behavior.

- Pro: back-compat. No existing workflows break.
- Con: violates the "explicit-required floor" principle we just locked in for Topology.
- Con: silent default that has had real-world failure modes (accidental public exposure of private code).

**Bot's recommendation: (Priv-default-1) strict explicit-required.** Consistency with Topology dominates. The friction is acceptable because `[default].private` in user-config is a one-time setup; after that, naked `init` works.

If you reject (1), I'd push toward (2) over (3) — security-safe defaults are worth a small back-compat break.

---

**Surface decision:**

**(Priv-surface)** Today's `--private` is boolean (presence = private). Two options:

- (a) Keep `--private` boolean. Need a `--public` peer for "explicit public" once defaulting becomes strict. Then `--private` / `--public` exactly-one-of when both default-required.
- (b) Switch to `--visibility public|private` (enum form). Single flag, explicit value. Future-extensible if GitHub Enterprise's `internal` visibility ever matters.

Bot's recommendation: **(a) keep `--private`, add `--public` peer.** Matches the `--por`/`--dual` peer-flag shape we used for Topology. Consistency wins; `--visibility` would be the only enum-spelling flag in the surface.

---

**Your call on Priv-default-{1,2,3} and Priv-surface-{a,b}.**

Two decisions in one message — let me unpack and check the broader implication.

**Privacy: default-public** ✓ (Priv-default-3, today's behavior).

Acknowledged. Surface stays as `--private` boolean, but we'll need a `--public` peer once `[default].private = true` is settable (so a user with private-default can spell "public" per invocation). Exactly-one-of, same shape as `--por` / `--dual`.

---

**Topology reversal: default-dual** — this is a meaningful reversal of the A1 / Topology decision. Let me make sure I understand the full implication.

Earlier we locked in: **explicit-required floor** for Topology — no implicit default; CLI / ENV / Local / Global / *Error*.

Now you're saying: **default-dual** — CLI / ENV / Local / Global / *Default (dual)*.

That's a principle change, not just a default-value change. And it cascades to **every axis we've negotiated under the "explicit-required" rule**. The cleaner restatement of the principle, given your two decisions:

> **Sensible defaults where natural; explicit-required only where the choice is user-specific.**

Applied per axis:

| Axis | Natural default? | Resolution end |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Topology | Yes — `dual` (the primary use case) | Default |
| Privacy | Yes — `public` | Default |
| Remote | Yes — `github-create` | Default (today's behavior) |
| Account | **No** — user-specific (which account?) | Error if no `[default].account` |
| Repo cat/val | **No** — depends on account | Error if no value resolvable |
| Copying / `--init-from*` | Yes — none (no copying) | Default |
| Scaffolding | Always on | Non-axis |

The pattern: **axes with a tool-wide natural default → default silently. Axes that require user-specific data (accounts, URLs) → error if not configured.**

This is actually a cleaner principle than the original "explicit-required everywhere" — and it matches what `resolve_repo` does today for account/category (errors only when those *specific* keys are missing, not when any key is missing).

Locked in. On to **Copying**.

The bulk of the design already lives in `notes/design-cli/copying.md` — pure copy, `--init-from*` flags, last-wins + warning on collision, canned writes suppressed when engaged, deferred dual validation. The natural default is **none** (no copying), which fits the "defaults where natural" principle cleanly.

A few open questions left from the design stub + new ones from the resolution chain we locked in afterward:

**(Copy-config) Is `--init-from*` config-settable?**

The resolution chain says every axis can pin in user-config. Concretely: `[default].init-from = ["~/templates/code/*", "~/templates/code/.gitignore"]` — a list of glob sources. Or per-account: `[account.work].init-from = [...]`.

Use case: "every new project I start gets these files copied in" — pin once, naked `vc-x1 init foo` always copies the template set.

- (a) **Yes** — config-pinnable as a list. Requires list-of-strings support in the user-config parser (today it's flat key/value; this is a small extension).
- (b) **No** — copying stays CLI-only. Config can't pin source globs.

Bot rec: **(a)**. Useful workflow, fits the resolution chain principle uniformly. Requires the parser tweak but that's small.

**(Copy-merge) Config-pinned + CLI-passed: merge or override?**

Special case because copying is *additive* (multiple `--init-from*` flags compose by union).

- (a) **CLI overrides config entirely** — `--init-from=...` on CLI means "ignore config, use only what I pass." Matches the broader chain rule (CLI > Config).
- (b) **Union** — CLI sources append to config sources; both apply.
- (c) **CLI-only when CLI present, but a `--init-from-also` extra-flag for "config + this too."**

Bot rec: **(a) CLI overrides config entirely.** Consistent with how every other axis works. If a user wants to layer their config-defaults with per-invocation extras, they spell the full list on CLI. Simple, predictable. (b) is tempting but breaks consistency — `--account home` doesn't merge with `[default].account = "work"`; it overrides. Copying should follow.

**(Copy-env) Env-var spelling.**

`VC_X1_INIT_FROM=<glob>` for a single value; `VC_X1_INIT_FROM_CODE`, `VC_X1_INIT_FROM_BOT` for per-side. Multiple sources? Probably colon-separated (`a:b:c`) — matches `PATH` convention, robust to spaces.

Bot rec: support env-vars per the same naming as elsewhere. **Colon-separated for multi-value.** This is detail-level; ack it and move on.

- **(Copy-subst) Variable substitution?** Bot rec: **no**, keep pure copy.
- **(Copy-symlinks) Preserve symlinks + exec bits (`cp -a` semantics) or flatten?** Bot rec: **preserve**.
- **(Copy-not-found) Source-not-found = hard error?** Bot rec: **yes, hard error** (typo protection). Empty-glob-expansion is fine.

> AGENT

Let me reflect this back to make sure I'm reading you right, then ask one ambiguity.

**Your proposed model:**

- **Baked-in default config** lives in the binary. Acts as the bottom-of-chain fallback.
- Resolution chain becomes: CLI > ENV > Local > Global > **Baked-in** (no "Error" floor for axes the baked-in covers).
- User can:
  - Get the workspace running with zero config (`vc-x1 init ./xyz` with no global config → baked-in defaults apply).
  - Dump the baked-in to a file, modify it, save as their global config.
  - Or save it locally / pass via `--init-from` for one-off use.
- **No per-axis "default" flags on CLI.** The CLI only carries per-invocation *values* (`--por`, `--public`, `--repo`); to change defaults you edit a config (local / global / baked-in-as-starting-point).
- **Future**: `linkme`-style registry lets multiple baked-in profiles ship with the binary; user picks which is "their default" at install or post-install.

If that's right, I think it's a **clean simplification** — and resolves a tension I'd been worried about (what does the error message say at the bottom of the chain when no global config exists). With a baked-in fallback, there's no error for the natural-default axes.

The chain naturally splits into two classes (matching the principle we already locked in):

- **Natural-default axes** (Topology, Privacy, Remote, Copying): baked-in supplies the default; no error possible.
- **User-specific keys** (account, repo URLs): baked-in can't supply these — your home GitHub URL isn't bakeable. These still error at the Global layer if unset. Resolution chain effectively ends at "Global > Error" for these.

---

**One ambiguity to resolve:**

You wrote: *"or specifying it using `vc-x1 --init-from=vc-config.toml`"*.

I'm not sure if `vc-config.toml` here means:

- (a) A **literal file path** the user has placed locally (e.g., `./vc-config.toml`) that they're passing to `--init-from`.
- (b) A **named template** baked into the binary, addressable by name — `--init-from=vc-config.toml` means "use the baked-in template called 'vc-config.toml'."

(a) keeps `--init-from` as just-a-glob-of-source-paths (its current design). (b) introduces a *named template registry* — `--init-from` accepts both file paths AND well-known names.

Bot rec: **(a)** — keep `--init-from` purely glob-of-paths. For "use the baked-in default," provide a dump command instead:

```
vc-x1 config dump > ~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml   # save as global
vc-x1 config dump > ./my-template.toml             # save locally to modify
vc-x1 init ./xyz --init-from=./my-template.toml    # use the modified file
```

The `linkme` future (multiple baked-in profiles) would extend `vc-x1 config dump` with a profile selector (`vc-x1 config dump --profile=rust-cli`), not extend `--init-from`. Keeps each mechanism focused.

---

**Confirm (a) and the model is locked**, or push toward (b).

Two follow-on calls if the model lands:

- **(BakedIn-content)** What does the baked-in default *contain*? Just the natural-default axes (`topology=dual`, `private=false`, `repo=?`)? Or also placeholders for user-specific keys (`[account.example].repo.category.remote = "<set-to-your-github-prefix>"`)? Bot rec: include placeholders with comments pointing at what the user needs to set. Acts as the implicit "first config" example.
- **(BakedIn-shape)** Is the baked-in config a *string constant* in Rust (`include_str!("default-config.toml")`)? Or generated programmatically? Bot rec: `include_str!` an actual `default-config.toml` file in the source tree — readable, diff-able, dump-able verbatim.

After those, on to **Scaffolding** (likely a quick non-axis confirmation).

confirm (a) and the model is locked

Model locked: baked-in default config as bottom-of-chain fallback, CLI carries only per-invocation values, `--init-from` stays glob-of-paths, `vc-x1 config dump` is the way to extract the baked-in.

The two follow-on details (baked-in content shape, `include_str!` vs generated) are implementation-cycle questions; the negotiated *contract* is what mattered, and that's done.

---

**Scaffolding** (jj init + `.gitignore`):

Today: always on. No flag. Was tentatively flagged as a *probable non-axis* in the audit doc.

Bot rec: **confirm non-axis, drop from the axis list at close-out.**

- `jj init` is not optional — the entire workspace model assumes jj.
- `.gitignore` content is a fixed canned list (`/target` `/.git` `/.jj` `/.vc-x1`); making it variable would be a templating feature (already subsumed by copying for the user-supplied case, and the canned write is suppressed when copying engages — so users *can* opt out of the canned `.gitignore` by passing `--init-from*`).

No knob needed. The axis isn't really an axis.

- `vc-x1 config dump` for extracting baked-in.
- Scaffolding = non-axis.

{

  "old_string": "### Gap list (input for close-out)",
  "new_string": "### Subcommand × parameter matrix\n\nThe designed-state at-a-glance view — for each\nsubcommand (rows), which parameter families it accepts\n(columns), and a short note on what the parameter does\nin that context.\n\nThis is the **post-equalization target**, not today's\nstate. Today many of these cells are gaps (per the audit\nfindings); the matrix captures what 0.62.0+ equalization\ncycles drive toward.\n\n**Parameter families:**\n\n- **T** — `--por` / `--dual` topology. *Creation* on\n  init/clone (writes the workspace shape); *runtime\n  override* on every other subcommand (forces\n  por-scoped operation).\n- **A/R** — `--account` + `--repo` (remote provider\n  resolution; user-config-keyed).\n- **Priv** — `--private` / `--public` (GitHub\n  visibility).\n- **CP** — `--init-from*` family (file copying into\n  the workspace).\n- **CFG** — `--config <path>` / `--global-config\n  <path>` (point at non-default config files).\n- **NO-CFG** — `--no-local-config` / `--no-global-config`\n  (skip layers in the resolution chain).\n- **SC** — runtime `--scope` / `-R` (per-invocation\n  repo selection on dual workspaces).\n\n**Cell legend:**\n\n- `✓` — accepted; default behavior.\n- `✓*` — accepted with caveat (see footnotes).\n- `—` — not applicable for this subcommand.\n- `(new)` — subcommand or capability added by the\n  0.62.0+ rollout.\n\n| Subcommand | T | A/R | Priv | CP | CFG | NO-CFG | SC |\n| --- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |\n| `init <TARGET>` | ✓ creation | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |\n| `clone <URL>` | ✓ creation | ✓ | ✓ | ✓* | ✓ | ✓ | — |\n| `push <BM>` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓* |\n| `sync` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |\n| `finalize` | — | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |\n| `chid` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |\n| `desc` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |\n| `show` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |\n| `list` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |\n| `validate-desc` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓* |\n| `fix-desc` | ✓ runtime | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓* |\n| `validate-todo` | — | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |\n| `fix-todo` | — | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |\n| `config dump` (new) | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |\n\n**Footnotes:**\n\n- `clone CP*` — copying on `clone` overlays *on top of*\n  the cloned content (same flag set, same collision\n  rules as `init`).\n- `push SC*` — push uses bookmark scoping rather than\n  `--scope`; the cell marks \"yes, push reads scope-ish\n  info\" without implying surface identity.\n- `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` `SC*` — these are the\n  desc-family outliers that bypass `for_each_repo`\n  today (per `## Commonality`); post-equalization\n  they route through scope like the rest of the\n  `CommonArgs` family.\n- `config dump` is a new subcommand to be added — emits\n  the baked-in default config so users can save and\n  modify it.\n\n**Topology column (`T`) — quick reference:**\n\n- *Creation* (init/clone): `--por` / `--dual` chooses\n  the workspace shape that gets written into\n  `.vc-config.toml` (dual only — por writes nothing).\n- *Runtime override* (other subcommands): `--por` at\n  invocation short-circuits `default_scope` →\n  `Scope([Code])` regardless of workspace contents.\n  Default-dual still applies at the workspace level\n  (the `.vc-config.toml` says dual, but the user is\n  asking for \"this command only, just my code\").\n- `finalize` has no T cell because it's invoked from\n  inside `push`'s workflow against a specific repo path\n  — no topology choice; the caller has already\n  resolved.\n\n### Gap list (input for close-out)"
}

859	
860	The designed-state at-a-glance view — for each

866	state. Today many of these cells are gaps (per the audit
867	findings); the matrix captures what 0.62.0+ equalization
868	cycles drive toward.
869	
870	**Parameter families:**
871	
872	- **T** — `--por` / `--dual` topology. *Creation* on
873	  init/clone (writes the workspace shape); *runtime
874	  override* on every other subcommand (forces
875	  por-scoped operation).
876	- **A/R** — `--account` + `--repo` (remote provider
877	  resolution; user-config-keyed).
878	- **Priv** — `--private` / `--public` (GitHub
879	  visibility).
880	- **CP** — `--init-from*` family (file copying into
881	  the workspace).
882	- **CFG** — `--config <path>` / `--global-config
883	  <path>` (point at non-default config files).
884	- **NO-CFG** — `--no-local-config` / `--no-global-config`
885	  (skip layers in the resolution chain).
886	- **SC** — runtime `--scope` / `-R` (per-invocation
887	  repo selection on dual workspaces).

893	- `—` — not applicable for this subcommand.
894	- `(new)` — subcommand or capability added by the
895	  0.62.0+ rollout.
896	
897	| Subcommand | T | A/R | Priv | CP | CFG | NO-CFG | SC |

And a use case is potentially when squashing a cycle into a single commit. Anyway --squash is "optional" logically if @ is empty. So again, I think finalize can be por|dual.
