774	### Resolution chain
775	
776	The per-axis sections above each say "CLI → user-config →
777	error." That's a sketch; the full resolution chain has
778	four layers plus an explicit-required floor. The same chain
779	applies to **every axis** and every field a config carries
780	(account, repo category/value, private, topology, …) —
781	nothing axis-specific.
782	
783	#### Layers (highest precedence first)
784	
785	1. **CLI flag** — `--por`, `--account`, `--private`,
786	   `--config <path>`, `--global-config <path>`, etc. The
787	   per-invocation surface. CLI is god — it wins over
788	   every other layer when present.
789	2. **Environment variable** — `VC_X1_<KEY>`. The
790	   per-session surface, shaped by `export` or one-shot
791	   prefix. Loses to CLI.
792	3. **Local config** — `./.vc-config.toml` (the workspace
793	   metadata file), or whatever `--config <path>` resolved
794	   to. Per-project durable intent. Loses to env-var and
795	   CLI; **may carry any field a CLI flag exposes** — no
796	   carve-outs (a local config can pin
797	   `[global] config-path` just as it pins `account`).
798	4. **Global config** — `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`
799	   (XDG-aware), or whatever `--global-config <path>` /
800	   `VC_X1_GLOBAL_CONFIG` / a local-config
801	   `[global] config-path` resolved to. Per-user durable
802	   defaults. Lowest-precedence config; loses to all
803	   above.
804	5. **Error** — "no `<key>` specified; set it via CLI,
805	   `VC_X1_<KEY>`, `./.vc-config.toml`, or
806	   `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`."
807	
808	Each layer is **optional**. Per-axis behavior under
809	the chain: the resolved value of a key is the value from
810	the highest layer that defines it; absence at every layer
811	falls to the error floor (or the axis's safe default if
812	one is defined — Copying defaults to `none` because
813	"no copying" is the only meaningful absence; no other
814	axis has a safe default today).
815	
816	#### Escape hatches
817	
818	- **`--no-local-config`** — skip layer 3 entirely for this
819	  invocation. Today functionally equivalent to runtime
820	  `--por` (because local config carries only topology
821	  metadata); add as a peer flag once local config gains
822	  non-topology fields.
823	- **`--no-global-config`** — skip layer 4 entirely.
824	  Necessary for tests / CI / "ignore my defaults this
825	  once."
826	
827	Both flags are layer-1 (CLI) — they can't themselves be
828	pinned by config, since their purpose is to ignore config.
829	
830	#### Redirection and cycles
831	
832	Both local and global config can redirect to a different
833	config file via `[global] config-path = "…"` (in either
834	file) and `[local] config-path = "…"` (in global).
835	**Circular redirections are user error** — the loader
836	keeps a visited-set and errors with the cycle path; no
837	attempt is made to break the cycle automatically.
838	
839	The bot thinks redirection in local pointing at a
840	custom global is the most useful case (a project that
841	ships a team-wide default file in the repo), and global
842	pointing at another global is mostly an unintended
843	consequence of letting the field exist universally.
844	Allowing it costs nothing.
845	
846	#### Env-var naming
847	
848	`VC_X1_<KEY>`, flat namespace, key matches the resolved
849	field name:
850	
851	| Axis | Env-var | Resolves to |
852	| --- | --- | --- |
853	| Topology | `VC_X1_TOPOLOGY` | `single` \| `dual` |
854	| Remote (account) | `VC_X1_ACCOUNT` | account name |
855	| Remote (repo) | `VC_X1_REPO` | `<cat>` or `<cat>=<val>` |
856	| Privacy | `VC_X1_PRIVATE` | `true` \| `false` |
857	| Copying | `VC_X1_INIT_FROM*` | colon-separated globs |
858	| — | `VC_X1_CONFIG` | local config path or `none` |
859	| — | `VC_X1_GLOBAL_CONFIG` | global config path |
860	| — | `VC_X1_NO_LOCAL_CONFIG` | `true` \| `false` |
861	| — | `VC_X1_NO_GLOBAL_CONFIG` | `true` \| `false` |
862	
863	Precedent in the codebase: `config.rs:113` already honors
864	`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; `test_tmp_root.rs` uses
865	`VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` / `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`. The pattern is
866	established; `VC_X1_*` just extends it.
867	
868	#### Por's view of the chain
869	
870	Following the decisions in this cycle:
871	
872	- **Workspace `.vc-config.toml`** (layer 3) — dual-only.
873	  Por never reads, never creates. Today's degenerate
874	  `[workspace] path = "/"` write under `--por` is residue
875	  from when `--por` was a half-implemented opt-out and
876	  should be dropped.
877	- **User-config `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`**
878	  (layer 4) — topology-neutral. Both por and dual consult
879	  it.
880	- **Runtime `--por`** — overrides workspace topology
881	  detection across every subcommand (not just
882	  `init`/`clone`). In a dual workspace, runtime `--por`
883	  short-circuits `default_scope` → `Scope([Code])` and
884	  ignores `.claude/`.
885	
886	### Subcommand × parameter matrix
887	
888	The designed-state at-a-glance view — for each
```

> DEVELOPER

Before moving on we've done some good work here, txs! But you're "medium" I'm thinking about asking "max" to review it. I previously was mostly working with xhigh but they felt slow. I liked this pace and I think you did a good job. Your thoughts on having xhigh or max to review?

> AGENT

Honest take: **yes, have max review, but as a focused review pass — not continued iteration.**

**Why the pace worked at medium:** design negotiation is a discovery process. You revealed assumptions I hadn't probed (the "--por is a tangled bundle" insight, the default-dual reversal, the baked-in default model), and I revealed framings you tightened (the "copying" naming, the A-prefix friction). That ping-pong only works at iteration cadences below ~30s per turn. Max/xhigh tend to spend longer thinking per turn — great for depth, slow for negotiation flow.

**Where max would add real value here:**

1. **Consistency check across ~1000 lines.** We made meaningful reversals mid-cycle (explicit-required → default-dual). Some prose written before the reversal may still be framed in the old model. Sections like Resolution-chain still pending rewrite; the Mapping subsection's tuples; the Connections-to-user-config paragraph. A max pass would spot the drift.
2. **Hidden contradictions.** Eight commits of accumulating decisions. Did decision N-5 conflict with decision N-1? I've been carrying state in my head, not auditing rigorously.
3. **Implementation-feasibility sniff test.** The design has open questions tagged "defer to implementation." Some may be load-bearing — a max pass with deeper code-context could flag the ones that'll bite.
4. **Completeness.** Are there axes / decisions / edge cases we missed entirely? Hard to know what I don't know.

**What I'd ask max to NOT do:**

- Rewrite for stylistic preferences. The doc has accumulated form, not house style.
- Re-negotiate decisions already locked in. They're decisions, not proposals.

**Concrete framing**: "Here's a pre-implementation design doc with negotiated decisions. Find internal contradictions, stale framings from earlier reversals, and load-bearing assumptions the design hasn't justified. Don't propose alternatives unless something is materially broken."

That focuses max on the *review* job rather than the *design* job, and protects what we built.

---

If you want, the natural seam is: **commit close-out at medium, then queue max to review the resulting committed state**. Cleaner reference point for them (a pushed branch) than a working-copy snapshot.

> DEVELOPER

finish close-out then queue max review, will I "queue max" or will you?

> AGENT

**You queue, not me.** Effort levels (`medium` / `xhigh` / `max`) are session settings controlled by `/effort <level>` — I can't change my own session's effort, and spawning a subagent doesn't carry effort across (it'd just spawn another medium agent under me).

Your options for the review:

1. **`/effort max` in this session**, then a review prompt. Easiest, but the model that designed the work also reviews it — same head, slower thinking. Risk: I may not catch my own blind spots.
2. **New session at max effort**. Fresh eyes on the committed branch. Better for independent review.
3. **`/ultrareview`** — multi-agent cloud review of the current branch (user-triggered, billed). Likely the right fit for "review this committed design comprehensively" — and the docs say to mention it when you ask about review. Run after close-out + push.

My recommendation: **`/ultrareview` after close-out lands and is pushed**. Fresh perspective, multi-agent so it catches different angles, and the committed branch is the natural reference point.

---

Now finishing close-out. Continuing with the Resolution-chain rewrite + Gap-list refresh + In Progress → chores migration.

  "new_string": "### Resolution chain\n\nThe per-axis sections above each say \"CLI → user-config →\ndefault (or error).\" That's a sketch; the full resolution\nchain has **five layers**, with the bottom varying per\naxis. CLI is god — wins over every\n   other layer when present. CLI carries *values* only;\n   there are **no per-axis \"default-X\" flags** (to change\n   a default, edit a config — local, global, or the\n   baked-in starting point).\n2. **Environment variable** — `VC_X1_<KEY>`. Layer 5\n(baked-in) is always present.\n\n#### Two-class resolution (per axis)\n\nNot every key has a natural default. The chain resolves\ndifferently for the two classes:\n\n- **Natural-default keys** — Topology, Privacy, Remote\n  category, Copying. The baked-in supplies a default\n  (`dual`, `public`, `github-create`, `none`). The chain\n  effectively ends at layer 5, never at \"error\" — naked\n  `vc-x1 init foo` with no user config works.\n- **User-specific keys** — `[default].account`, account\n  repo URLs (`[account.<a>].repo.category.remote = \"...\"`),\n  account repo fixtures. The baked-in cannot supply these\n  — there's no universal `git@github.com:<your-handle>`\n  to bake in. The chain ends at \"Error\" if layers 1–4\n  don't supply: *\"no `<key>` specified; set it in\n  `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` or via the appropriate\n  CLI flag.\"*\n\nThe principle is **\"defaults where natural; errors only\nfor user-specific keys\"** — it matches what `resolve_repo`\nalready does today for `--account` / `--repo` (errors\nspecifically when those keys are missing, not when any\nkey is missing)."

183	
184	## docs: por/dual parity audit (0.61.0)
185	
186	Commits: TBD (backfills at next cycle's Preparation)
187	
188	Audit cycle for T5 (`por/dual parity + dual → por`).
189	Produces `notes/design-cli/por-dual-parity-audit.md` — a per-area
190	inventory of where `dual` (code + `.claude/` companion
191	cross-linked by `ochid:`) gets a privileged code path
192	over `por` (plain single repo). The audit's output is
193	input for future equalization cycles; no code changes
194	land in 0.61.0 itself.
195	
196	The decision to audit first (vs. starting from a user-config
197	topology default) is captured in the
198	[problem statement](../todo.md#in-progress): a defaults
199	knob in front of asymmetric paths funnels users onto a
200	still-bolted-on `por` path; equalize first, default later.
201	
202	### Audit scope
203	
204	Areas the audit walk inspects, each producing its own
205	section in `notes/design-cli/por-dual-parity-audit.md`:
206	
207	- **`options_flags/por.rs`** — the `--por` gate itself;
208	  how it's threaded through commands.
209	- **`init`** — `.vc-config.toml` write, `.claude/`
210	  companion creation, template seeding, push retries.
211	- **`clone`** — mirror of init for an existing remote.
212	- **`push`** — two-repo coordination, `ochid:`
213	  composition, finalize handoff.
214	- **`sync`** — two-repo pull dance, `-R` flag wiring.
215	- **`finalize`** — `.claude` cadence (one push = one
216	  `.claude` commit), squash + push detach.
217	- **`show` / `chid` / `desc` helpers / `validate-desc`
218	  / `fix-desc`** — anything that reads or writes ochid
219	  trailers or `.claude` state.
220	- **Tests and fixtures** — where `FixturePor` and the
221	  dual fixture diverge in coverage.
222	
223	The audit doesn't propose fixes inline; it produces a
224	parity-gap table that close-out turns into ranked
225	follow-up Todo entries.
226	
227	# References
228	
229	[1]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/a199d062ff6e "a199d062ff6e88b5e2d87d57551d1c60e75b073b"
230	[2]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/e67e44b8e1c5 "e67e44b8e1c55b8e7c33087b8f2184df87181885"

The audit's output is\ninput for future equalization cycles; no code changes\nland in 0.61.0 itself.\n\nThe decision to audit first (vs. starting from a user-config\ntopology default) is captured in the\n[problem statement](../todo.md#in-progress): a defaults\nknob in front of asymmetric paths funnels users onto a\nstill-bolted-on `por` path; equalize first, default later.\n\n### Audit scope\n\nAreas the audit walk inspects, each producing its own\nsection in `notes/design-cli/por-dual-parity-audit.md`:\n\n- **`options_flags/por.rs`** — the `--por` gate itself;\n  how it's threaded through commands.\n- **`init`** — `.vc-config.toml` write, `.claude/`\n  companion creation, template seeding, push retries.\n- **`clone`** — mirror of init for an existing remote.\n- **`push`** — two-repo coordination, `ochid:`\n  composition, finalize handoff.\n- **`sync`** — two-repo pull dance, `-R` flag wiring.\n- **`finalize`** — `.claude` cadence (one push = one\n  `.claude` commit), squash + push detach.\n- **`show` / `chid` / `desc` helpers / `validate-desc`\n  / `fix-desc`** — anything that reads or writes ochid\n  trailers or `.claude` state.\n- **Tests and fixtures** — where `FixturePor` and the\n  dual fixture diverge in coverage.\n\nThe audit doesn't propose fixes inline; it produces a\nparity-gap table that close-out turns into ranked\nfollow-up Todo entries.",
  "new_string": "## docs: por/dual parity design (0.61.0)\n\nCommits: TBD (backfills at next cycle's Preparation)\n\nDesign cycle for T5 (`por/dual parity + dual → por`).\nScope grew over eight commits from an audit to a full\nCLI design — the audit doc became the canonical record\nfor both the parity divergences (where dual gets\nprivileged code paths over por) and the negotiated\ndesigned surface (feature axes, decisions per axis,\nresolution chain with baked-in default, subcommand ×\nparameter matrix, copying mechanism for file seeding).\nOutput is input for 0.62.0+ equalization cycles. No\ncode changes land in 0.61.0 itself.\n\nThe cycle started as \"audit + ranked Todos for future\nfixes\" and grew when the audit's findings made clear\nthat defaulting topology (the user's original framing)\nwouldn't be safe before equalizing the underlying paths,\nand that `--por` itself was a tangled bundle of\nindependent feature axes (topology, `.vc-config.toml`\nwrite, remote, privacy, copying, scaffolding) rather\nthan a single toggle. The In Progress block (moved\nbelow) records the problem statement and the as-built\nladder.\n\n### Problem statement (preserved from In Progress)\n\nAudit where `dual` (code + `.claude/` companion, cross-\nlinked by `ochid:`) gets privileged code paths vs `por`\n(plain single repo). Goal: produce a parity-gap document\nthat drives future parity-equalization cycles. T5 in\n`## Todo` is the parent design item; this cycle is the\naudit step that precedes any code-level equalization.\n\nThe user proposed starting from `~/.config/vc-x1/config.\ntoml` to make the topology choice user-configurable; the\nbot counter-proposed that a defaults knob shouldn't ship\nbefore the underlying paths are equalized, and the user\nagreed — audit first, equalize next, then expose\ndefaults.\n\n### Scope arc\n\nThe cycle's scope expanded mid-flight three times — each\ntime a new pass revealed something the prior pass didn't:\n\n- **Audit walk (`-1`)** found that `push` is fully dual-\n  baked, `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` are dual-required\n  outliers, and `sync` already implements the topology-\n  neutral pattern via `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos`.\n- **Commonality (`-2`)** inverted the view and showed\n  that the codebase *already has* the right pattern;\n  half the subcommands route through it. The \"por is\n  bolted on\" framing inverted: por is bolted on\n  everywhere the scope pattern wasn't applied.\n- **Feature axes (`-3`)** decomposed `--por` from a\n  topology toggle into six axes: Topology,\n  `.vc-config.toml` write, Remote, Privacy, Copying,\n  Scaffolding. Plus a 4-layer resolution chain (CLI >\n  ENV > Local > Global) — later extended to 5 layers\n  with a baked-in default at the bottom.\n- **A2 collapse + copying stub (`-4`)** showed that the\n  `.vc-config.toml` write axis is degenerate\n  post-Topology, and the broader file-copying mechanism\n  (`--init-from*`) subsumes both `--config` and\n  `--gitignore` flags. `notes/design-cli/copying.md`\n  captures the design.\n- **`design-cli/` subdir (`-5`)** grouped the\n  accumulating CLI design notes (copying.md,\n  por-dual-parity.md, and the cycle's audit doc) under\n  one home.\n- **Decisions + matrix (`-6`)** captured per-axis\n  negotiation outcomes as `**Decisions (0.61.0):**`\n  blocks inline, plus the subcommand × parameter matrix\n  the user requested.\n- **Label rename + ledger + finalize fix (`-7`)** dropped\n  `A1`–`A6` prefixes for descriptive axis names, added\n  a clean acronym-to-axis ledger to the matrix, and\n  acknowledged at the top of the doc that this is a\n  pre-implementation snapshot — the final shape will\n  differ.\n\n### Mid-cycle reversals worth noting\n\nTwo negotiated reversals shaped the final design:\n\n- **Topology floor: strict-explicit-required → default-\n  dual.** Initial decision was \"no implicit default;\n  error if not specified.\" User reframed to \"sensible\n  defaults where natural, errors only for user-specific\n  keys\" — landing default-dual as the natural primary\n  use case. Cascaded to Privacy (default-public),\n  Remote (default-github-create), Copying (default-\n  none).\n- **Baked-in default config as bottom-of-chain.** Closes\n  the \"what does the error message say at the bottom\"\n  question for natural-default axes — there's always a\n  fallback from the binary's `default-config.toml`. New\n  `vc-x1 config dump` subcommand exposes it for users\n  to save and modify.\n\n### Status note (in the audit doc top)\n\nThe audit doc carries an explicit \"this design is pre-\nimplementation; the final shape will differ\" note. Future\nreaders (including the bot in 0.62.0 cycles) should treat\nthe design as a starting position, update it when code\nreveals divergence, and not bend code to fit stale\nsketches.\n\n### As-built ladder\n\n- 0.61.0-0 Preparation — backfill 0.60.0 chores\n  `Commits:` ref; bump Cargo.toml; pick up T5 into\n  `## In Progress`; open chores section; lay out the\n  audit scope.\n- 0.61.0-1 Audit walk — produce\n  `notes/design-cli/por-dual-parity-audit.md` with 8\n  sections inventorying dual-privileged code paths\n  (per-area: files touched, divergence, severity,\n  equalization sketch).\n- 0.61.0-2 Commonality analysis — append `## Commonality`\n  inverting the view (per-subcommand shared / dual-only\n  / por-only buckets, ranked equalization candidates).\n- 0.61.0-3 Feature-axes decomposition — append\n  `## Feature axes` (six axes with current/target\n  states); 4-layer resolution chain (CLI > ENV >\n  Local > Global > Error); escape hatches; env-var\n  table; por's view of the chain.\n- 0.61.0-4 A2 collapse + copying stub — `.vc-config.toml`\n  write axis collapsed under Topology decisions;\n  `notes/design-cli/copying.md` design stub captures\n  the broader file-copying mechanism that subsumes\n  `--config`, `--gitignore`, and `--use-template`.\n- 0.61.0-5 `design-cli/` subdir — group three CLI design\n  notes (copying, por-dual-parity, the audit doc) into\n  `notes/design-cli/`; update cross-references; leave\n  historical chores untouched.\n- 0.61.0-6 Captures + matrix — `**Decisions (0.61.0):**`\n  blocks under each axis section; new\n  `### Subcommand × parameter matrix` (14 subcommand\n  rows × 7 parameter families with ledger + legend +\n  footnotes).\n- 0.61.0-7 Label rename + ledger + status — drop\n  `A1`–`A6` prefixes for descriptive axis names; refactor\n  matrix parameter-families intro into clean ledger\n  table; correct `finalize` matrix row (T runtime + SC\n  for standalone use); add `## Status` note framing\n  this as pre-implementation.\n- 0.61.0 Close-out — Resolution-chain rewrite for the\n  baked-in-default 5-layer model + two-class principle;\n  Gap-list refresh (14 implementation-cycle gaps for\n  0.62.0+ to seed Todos from); In Progress → chores\n  migration; `## Done` entry; `## In Progress` reset.\n\n### Outcome — what's seeded for 0.62.0+\n\nThe refreshed Gap list in the audit doc carries 14\nimplementation gaps. The close-out commit promotes one\ninto `## Todo` (T7: `validate-desc` / `fix-desc`\nequalization — cheapest concrete equalization, validates\nthe topology-from-config rule via prototype). The rest\nlive in the audit doc's Gap list for future Preparation\npasses to pick up."
